Built against the real `tenant_service_requests` system (`ServiceRequestType`: `MAINTENANCE|ROOM_CHANGE|CLEANING|LOST_KEY|VISITOR_PASS|EXTRA_MATTRESS`), not the unused `complaints` Prisma table.
  2. **Payment-standing tier copy does not literally reproduce the mockup's "New tenant"/"Getting started" framing.** The design's one demo state shows a tenant starting at the bottom tier and building up over time. The real scoring in `tenant-score-service.ts` works the opposite way — every tenant starts at 100 (`EXCELLENT`) and is only deducted for late payments, confirmed live against a fresh dev-DB test tenant (score 100, grade `EXCELLENT`, immediately after creation with zero payment history). Copying the mockup's specific narrative verbatim would have shown a genuinely excellent-standing tenant a message implying they had something to prove. Tier labels/copy were written to reflect the real score semantics instead (`Building`/`Fair`/`Good`/`Excellent`, mapped from `HIGH_RISK|NEEDS_ATTENTION|FAIR|GOOD|EXCELLENT`).
- **Consequences:** the tenant dashboard's IA matches the design source exactly (5 tabs, no more, no less) while its financial-standing copy is honest about a system the design's own JS never modeled at all (the mockup's state values are illustrative demo data, not real product logic, per the standing "treat `.dc.html` interactivity as illustrative only" rule). A future session building a literal "New tenant" onboarding-progress feature (distinct from payment-risk standing) would be a genuinely new feature, not a restoration of something this pass removed.
- **Related:** [[Features#Tenant dashboard — pixel-fidelity rebuild (2026-08-14)|Features]], [[Changelog]], [[Bugs]].

## ADR-069: Tenant-proposes/owner-approves profile edits — new parallel routes, not a `ChangeManagementFacade` extension

- **Date:** 2026-08-14
- **Status:** accepted
- **Context:** user wanted tenant self-edits to their phone/email/address/DOB gated on owner approval, with everything else saving directly. Initial plan (told to the user, then corrected) was to reuse the existing `change_requests` table's infrastructure — it looked purpose-built (`change_type: 'profile_update'` already existed, `ChangeApprovalLevel.L1 = "Owner only"` reads like an exact fit). On inspection, `ChangeManagementFacade.propose()`/`approve()`/`reject()` are hardwired the *opposite* direction: they power a real, live owner-side feature ("Request Change" on the owner's Tenant Profile page → tenant must approve), with `actor_role` hardcoded per method and `approve()`/`reject()` throwing `FORBIDDEN` unless the caller is the tenant on that specific `tenant_id`. There is no existing tenant-proposes/owner-approves path anywhere in the codebase.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Reuse the `change_requests`/`change_request_events` tables** (schema/audit consistency, and `entity_type`/`change_category`/`approval_level` already have room for this shape) **but do not touch `ChangeManagementFacade`.** New rows are tagged `change_type: 'tenant_self_service_update'`, distinguishing them from the facade's `'profile_update'`/`'contract_amendment'`/`'administrative_correction'` rows in the same table.
  2. **New, separate route set** (`app/api/tenants/me/profile-requests`, `app/api/owner/profile-requests[/[id]/approve|reject]`) with its own authorization and apply-logic, rather than adding a role parameter to the facade's methods. Extending the facade risked subtly changing behavior for the live owner-initiated flow (its `approve()`/`reject()` hardcode `actor_role: "tenant"` in the audit log, assume `tenant_id` on the row is who must approve — none of that is true for this direction).
  3. **Enforcement lives in the service layer, not just routing.** `updateTenantSelfProfile()` rejects governed fields outright (400) even though the new frontend never sends them — defense in depth, since this method has two callers (`/api/tenants/me/profile` and the older `/api/tenants/profile`) and nothing stops a third from appearing later.
  4. **Governed field list is exactly what the user specified**: primary phone, personal email, permanent address, date of birth. Not extended to PAN/blood group/nationality (new fields added the same day) or emergency/guardian contact info, since the user didn't name them and there's no other signal they need the same protection.
- **Consequences:** two directions of "change request" now coexist in one table, disambiguated only by `change_type` — anything reading `change_requests` broadly (e.g. a future admin dashboard) needs to filter or label by direction, not assume one meaning. The primary-phone OTP-verification block inside `updateTenantSelfProfile` is now unreachable dead code for this call path (see [[Business-Rules]]) — left in place rather than removed, flagged for a future cleanup pass. No owner-side review UI existed before this change; a new one was built (`features/owner-profile-requests`, routed at `/owner/profile-requests`, linked from the Alerts → Requests tab) rather than deferring it, per explicit user direction after being told the fuller scope.
- **Related:** [[Database]], [[Business-Rules#Tenant self-service profile edits — governed vs. direct fields (2026-08-14, narrowed same day)|Business-Rules]], [[APIs]], [[Features]], [[Changelog]].

**Amendment, same day (2026-08-14):** point 4's governed field list turned out to be one step ahead of the user's actual intent — after comparing the resulting UI against the design source, the user gave a final, explicit, narrower list: *only* phone and email require approval; date of birth and address (both in the original list) go back to direct-editable, same as everything else. `permanent_address`/`date_of_birth` removed from `GOVERNED_FIELDS`/the profile-requests routes/`GOVERNED_PROFILE_FIELDS`. This also prompted restoring the read-only card visual design (view/edit toggle in `ProfileEditScreen.tsx`, see [[Bugs]] for the field-mapping and validator-wiring bugs found while implementing it) — the interim always-open-form version was a deviation from `Stayo Tenant.dc.html` the user explicitly rejected, not a separate decision.

## ADR-070: Tenant activation — Identity now precedes Agreement, a real backend step-order swap

- **Date:** 2026-08-14
- **Status:** accepted
- **Context:** the tenant activation wizard's design source turned out to have two versions in this repo: `Stayo SaaS redesign/Stayo Onboarding.dc.html` (used for the wizard's original 2026-08-12/13 build) and a second, different folder, `stayo onbaording/Stayo Onboarding.dc.html`, which the user identified as the one that should have been used. Diffing the two showed a real flow change, not a styling revision: the newer source shows Identity (gender/DOB/guardian/emergency/photo, plus mobile-OTP/Gmail moved down from the old Welcome screen) *before* Agreement, whereas the shipped wizard — and the backend's `activation-workflow-service.ts` — required Agreement first. Confirmed via full read of the backend service: `AGREEMENT`-before-`PROFILE` was a hard-enforced server-side gate (`assertTransition()`, HTTP 409 `INVALID_TRANSITION`), not a UI convention, and confirmed there is no data dependency either direction (Agreement's `content_snapshot` never reads Profile-collected fields, and `saveProfile()` never reads the signed agreement) — the old order was a pure policy choice, safe to reverse.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Swap `PROFILE` and `AGREEMENT` in the backend's required step sequence.** `agreement-requirement.ts`'s `ALL_ACTIVATION_STEPS` reordered to `ACCOUNT → RULES → PROFILE → AGREEMENT → ACTIVATE`; `activation-workflow-service.ts`'s `assertTransition()` and `blockedSteps()` updated symmetrically — `PROFILE` no longer requires `agreement_signed`, `AGREEMENT` now requires `profile_completed` instead. The `RULES` auto-accept side effect (runs before every `assertTransition()` call) and `ACTIVATE`'s final re-check are unaffected — both already independent of ordering.
  2. **Merge the Welcome and Identity screens' React components** (`AccountStep.tsx` + `ProfileStep.tsx` → `WelcomeIdentityStep.tsx`) rather than just reordering two separate screens, matching the design source exactly: Welcome trims to Room Allocation + Billing Cycle only (no backend call — a local-only UI phase), and Identity gains the mobile-OTP/Gmail fields alongside photo/gender/DOB/guardian/emergency, submitting the `ACCOUNT` and `PROFILE` backend steps chained behind one tap — the same one-component-two-backend-steps pattern `AgreementStep.tsx` already used for `RULES`+`AGREEMENT`. Chosen over a frontend-only relabel (which would have desynced the progress track from the actual screen shown, since Profile and Agreement remain genuinely distinct backend steps) after being put to the user directly as a scope question.
  3. **`ActivationProgress.tsx`'s node order reordered to match** (`Welcome → Identity → Agreement → Account → Move In`) — a single array reorder, since the track's draw order, done/active state, and click-gating all key off the same `ALL_NODES` array index.
  4. **Dual-brand header, real hostel name, and decorative "Checking in…/Checking out…" speech bubbles** ported from the same newer design source, per explicit user approval — the Stayo icon × hostel-logo/initials badge replaces the old Stayo-only header on both the intro screen and the wizard chrome.
- **Consequences:** a tenant now completes their identity profile before reviewing and signing the residency agreement — the opposite of the original design intent this wizard first shipped with. No functional regression: OTP flows, guardian verification, signature capture, and activation submission are all carried over unchanged, only their step order and screen grouping changed. Any future code reading `ALL_ACTIVATION_STEPS`/`assertTransition`/`blockedSteps` order assumptions (e.g. a report or admin view hardcoding the old sequence) needs updating — grepped for other consumers of step order at the time of this change; none found beyond the files listed above.
- **Related:** [[Business-Rules#Whether a tenant must sign an agreement|Business-Rules]], [[Features]], [[Changelog]].

**Amendment, same day (2026-08-14):** a follow-up comparison against a real screenshot of the design source surfaced three more fields the newer design's Identity screen doesn't show at all — a Profile Type selector, a Guardian Relation dropdown, and a 4th "Prefer not to say" gender option — all carried over from the pre-ADR-070 `ProfileStep.tsx`. Put to the user directly (this contradicted the "keep extra backend fields, restyle" policy confirmed twice earlier the same day); the user chose exact fidelity over the standing policy for these three specifically. Profile Type was safe to drop outright: `tenants.profile_type` already defaults to `STUDENT` at the schema level, is never set during the owner's invite flow, and is directly editable later from the tenant's own portal profile (`tenant-service.ts`'s direct-editable field list) — so removing the selector here loses no capability, it just moves the moment a working-professional tenant corrects it to after activation. Guardian Relation could not simply be deleted, though: `saveProfile()` hard-required it for student profiles, and — since Profile Type dropping means every tenant now defaults to `STUDENT` — every activation would have needed it, with nothing earlier in the (now-reordered) flow left to collect it (the only other place relation is asked, the Agreement step's guardian-signature modal, runs *after* Profile and is optional). Resolved by relaxing `saveProfile()`'s requirement — guardian name and verified phone stay required for students, relation is no longer required at this step and is filled in later only if a guardian actually co-signs the agreement (both `tenants.guardian_relation` and `agreements.guardian_relation` are already nullable columns, so this is schema-safe). Gender trimmed to the design's 3 options (Male/Female/Other); `"Prefer not to say"` is no longer selectable through this flow (still a valid backend value, just not offered here).

**Second amendment, same day (2026-08-14):** Emergency Contact removed from the Identity screen outright, by explicit direct instruction (no design-fidelity ambiguity this time — the field simply isn't wanted here). Unlike Profile Type/Guardian Relation, this one had no other capture point anywhere in the activation flow, so it's a straightforward removal, not a relocation. `saveProfile()`'s unconditional `emergencyPhone` requirement was relaxed the same way Guardian Relation's was: an emergency number is still validated for format *if* one is ever supplied (defense in depth for other callers of this method), but no longer required to complete the `PROFILE` step. `tenants.phone_3`/`emergency_contact` stay nullable columns — schema-safe. Emergency contact info, where still needed operationally, is collected later from the tenant portal profile (already a direct-editable field per `tenant-service.ts`), not during activation.

## ADR-071: `/` becomes an audience chooser; the owner marketing page moves to `/owners`

- **Date:** 2026-08-14
- **Status:** accepted
- **Context:** Stayo serves two audiences with almost nothing in common — students looking for a bed, and owners looking for software — but `/` was the owner marketing page (`LandingPage`, ADR-035's login surface). A student arriving at the root from a search or a shared link landed in a pitch about occupancy dashboards and late-fee automation. `Stayo Welcome.dc.html` (Claude Design project `3f2fbde6`) designed the fork: a full-viewport diagonal seam, tenant above / owner below, that yields to whichever side you hover or tap.
- **Decision:**
  1. **`/` renders `WelcomePage`; `LandingPage` moves to `/owners`.** "Start free" commits to `/owners`. `/login` still renders `LandingPage` with the popup open, unchanged — ADR-035 is untouched.
  2. **Picking a side is reversible — the choice is a mode, not an identity.** The Stayo logo on `LandingPage` (previously an in-page `#top` anchor, since this page *was* `/`) is now a real `<Link to="/">`, and the marketing-chrome logos on `MarketingLayout`/`CompanyPage` point at `/` too. **Rule: logos mean "front door" and go to `/`; content CTAs and route guards go to `/owners`.** The tenant side gets its own way back when that side is designed — there is no tenant page to return *from* yet.
  3. **Auth failures go to `/login`, not `/`.** `ProtectedRoute` (no session) and `ProtectedTenantRoute` previously bounced to `/`; asking someone whose session just expired whether they're a student or an owner — instead of letting them sign back in — loses the thread. The *authorization* failure (signed in, wrong role) still goes to `/`, because there we genuinely don't know which surface they belong on and the chooser is exactly that question.
  4. **`/` renders for everyone, including signed-in owners.** An earlier revision of this ADR had `WelcomePage` hard-`<Navigate>` a signed-in owner to `/owners`. That was **reverted the same day**: it made the tenant side permanently unreachable for the exact person who most needs it — an owner cannot browse hostel listings if the front door ejects them — and it also defeated point 2, since a signed-in owner clicking the logo would have bounced straight back. The session now changes the owner CTA's *wording and destination* ("Go to dashboard" → `/owner/home` for an owner with a hostel; "Start free" → `/owners` otherwise) instead of removing the choice. Persisting the pick in `localStorage` was considered and rejected for the same reason: it makes `/` unpredictable and needs an escape hatch.
  5. **Choosing "owner" is treated as an answer, not just a navigation.** `/owners` carries an `OwnerEnquiryPrompt` asking "Are you a hostel owner?" — which is the exact question the visitor just answered by tapping the owner panel. `WelcomePage` therefore hands `{ declaredOwnerIntent: true }` to `/owners` via router state (matching how `OwnerLeadInvitePage` already passes prefill to `/onboarding`), and `LandingPage` opens `HostelLeadModal` on the first paint while suppressing the prompt. `declaredOwnerIntent` is a new **optional** field on `EnquiryPromptInput`, checked before every other rule and ahead of scroll depth — the question is answered, not merely badly timed. Optional so the callers with no such signal are unchanged, with a test pinning absent ≡ `false`. The state is consumed with a `replace` navigation so history back/forward can't re-open the conversation.
  6. **The tenant panel is deliberately inert.** It expands and reads its pitch, but "Browse hostels" is a non-navigating `aria-disabled` pill labelled "Coming soon" — the hostel-browsing page it would open (cluster listings) is designed later, and routing into a dead end is worse than saying so. Deliberately **not** a separate tenant landing page: per explicit product direction there will be one browsing page, not a marketing page plus a browse page.
  7. **Two deviations from the design source, both deliberate.** The 2.65s intro splash was cut to 1.6s, shown once per browser session (`sessionStorage: stayo.welcome.splashSeen`) and skipped entirely under `prefers-reduced-motion` — fine on a canvas, far too long as a front door on every visit. The commit overlay's rotating ring was replaced with `StayoLoader`: rotating spinners are deliberately absent app-wide (see `shared/ui/brand/index.ts`), every wait is the mark's four windows lighting clockwise.
- **Consequences:** **`/` is no longer the crawlable owner-acquisition page, and this has a real SEO cost** — the root's accumulated ranking for owner keywords does not transfer to `/owners` cleanly. `sitemap.xml` now gives `/owners` the 1.0 priority and demotes `/` to 0.7; a 301 or a canonical from `/` to `/owners` was *not* added, because `/` is a genuine distinct page now, not a duplicate. Worth revisiting once the tenant marketplace ships and `/` has its own reason to rank. The brand palette in `WelcomePage` is hard-coded rather than themed, same as `StayoLoadingScreen` — it renders before any `[data-app-theme]` shell exists, so there is no token scope to inherit.
- **Related:** [[Features#Welcome screen — the audience fork at `/` (2026-08-14)|Features]], [[Frontend]], [[Changelog]], [[Decisions#ADR-035|ADR-035]] (the one-login-surface rule `/login` still follows).

**Amendment (2026-08-15):** point 6 is superseded. The tenant panel is no longer inert — "Browse hostels" commits to `/discover`, the marketplace built in [[Decisions#ADR-073|ADR-073]]. The rest of point 6 stands, including its "one browsing page, not a marketing page plus a browse page" direction: Discover *is* that one page. The consequences note above anticipated this — `/` now has a tenant-side reason to rank, so the deferred question of whether `/` deserves its own SEO treatment (rather than the 0.7 demotion it got when it led nowhere for half its visitors) is now live and still unanswered.

## ADR-072: The onboarding design's animations are part of the spec, and the deviations from it are enumerated rather than assumed

- **Date:** 2026-08-15
- **Status:** accepted
- **Context:** the tenant activation flow was built (2026-08-12/13) and re-cut (2026-08-14, [[Decisions#ADR-070|ADR-070]]) against `Stayo Onboarding.dc.html`, but both passes ported *layout and color* and treated motion as decoration. The user re-supplied the design straight from Claude Design project `3f2fbde6` with the explicit framing that it is approved **"with all animations and all"**, and that minute differences matter. A line-by-line diff against the source found the gaps clustered in exactly the places a layout-first port loses: motion (the idle avatar, the sign-row pulse, the ambient audio), the pieces of chrome that only exist as behaviour (the sticky action bar), and the one screen that had been satisfied with a stand-in (the signature pad).
- **Decision:**
  1. **Motion is ported at the same fidelity as layout.** Every keyframe in the source's `<style>` block that the flow uses now exists in one co-located `onboarding.css` with the source's own names and timings, and the states that drive them are wired: the journey avatar's 11s `bored` sit-and-doze, the unsigned signature row's `sigGlow` pulse and `nudgeX` arrow, the intro's ambient soundtrack. Where the source's own markup makes an animation a no-op — the walk/breathe transform sits on a wrapper whose absolutely-positioned parts escape to the grandparent, so the figure never actually bobs — **the intent is implemented, not the bug**, and the departure is commented at the site.
  2. **The full-screen signature sheet is built for this flow, not borrowed.** `steps/SignatureSheet.tsx` owns its canvas via pointer events. It deliberately does *not* reuse `@shared/ui/inputs`'s `SignaturePad`, which carries owner-side chrome the design has none of (a fullscreen-expand control, rotate buttons, an indigo confirm, a fixed-height box) — wrapping it was what produced the generic slate dialog this replaces. The one behaviour kept from the old path: strokes are flattened onto white on export, because the agreement PDF composites the image over a light page.
  3. **The sticky action bar is layout, not styling.** The design pins the primary action; rendering it inline at the end of each step's content put it below the fold on every step but the last. `StepActionBar` is shared and `ActivationLayout` reserves the design's 108px for it, so no step can regress this independently.
  4. **Every departure from the design is enumerated, with its reason, rather than left to be re-discovered.** The standing ones: 6-digit OTP (the backend issues six), native `<select>`/`<input type="date">` in place of the source's tap-to-cycle rows (a11y and platform pickers), the 5 rules acknowledgements and the pre-activation verification checklist (backend-required and backend-useful respectively; the source's mockup has no UI for either), an 8-character password minimum against the source's 6, the guardian lock/Modify affordance (the name syncs from agreement signing), and "Last step" where the source literally renders "0 steps left". Ported *to* the design against prior local judgement: step 1's heading is "Welcome to Stayo" (the hostel name is already in the flow header), the guardian signed row reads "Guardian signature applied" without the name, and step 4's primary is "Create Account & Continue".
  5. **Dead state in the source is not ported.** The design defines an `activated` full-screen success overlay and a `checklist` array that nothing ever renders or sets. `WelcomeSummaryStep` is the real completion screen; neither is recreated.
- **Consequences:** the flow now carries motion that must survive future edits — a step that stops using `StepActionBar`, or a CSS consolidation that drops `onboarding.css` from `ActivationLayout`, silently loses part of the approved design with nothing failing. There is no automated check for this (the frontend has no test suite), so it rests on the co-location and the comments. The ambient audio only starts on an explicit tap, so autoplay policy is not a factor; the `AudioContext` is closed on unmount. `SignatureSheet` is now the only signature capture in the tenant flow — the frozen `src/portal/pages/ActivateAccountPage.tsx` keeps its own local copies and is untouched.
- **Related:** [[Decisions#ADR-070|ADR-070]] (the step-order swap this builds on), [[Features]], [[Bugs]], [[Changelog]], [[Frontend]].

**Amendment (2026-08-15):** point 4's list of "standing departures" was too permissive in one place. Step 4's onboarding verification checklist and signed-agreement preview card were listed there as backend-useful surfaces kept and restyled; the user rejected that on sight of a production screenshot and they are now **removed**, along with `AgreementPreviewModal`. The reason is stronger than fidelity: both restated state from earlier stages rather than reading it, and both fell back to a plausible-looking value when their source was empty — so on an `agreement_required: false` hostel they asserted a signature that did not exist (see [[Bugs]]). **The rule this establishes: a surface that summarises another stage must read that stage's state and render nothing when it is absent — never a fallback.** Auditing the flow for that shape found two more (a hardcoded "Step 1 of 5" contradicting the 4-stage track, and guardian copy crediting an Agreement step that this ADR's parent moved to run *later*), both fixed. Three things were *not* removed despite having no design counterpart, and the distinction is deliberate: the Step 3 acknowledgements (removing the UI would auto-accept a legal consent on the tenant's behalf — a consent decision, not a design one), the `ACADEMIC_YEARLY` billing option (a real selectable value; removing it removes a capability), and Step 4's activation progress bar (the design has no async at all, so it has nothing to say about a multi-second call). Design fidelity governs *presentation*; it does not govern consent, capability, or the handling of real latency. **Follow-up the same day:** with stage-by-stage screenshots supplied, the acknowledgements were re-examined and the reasoning above verified directly against `acceptRules()` — it validates all five and writes an IP/user-agent-stamped `tenant_policy_acceptances` row, so the UI is load-bearing, not decorative. Rather than keep them as a second card beside the design's single document box, they became a closing numbered section *inside* it; the design's own box already elides sections 2–5, so a final "7." is at home there. **The general form: when a required surface has no design counterpart, prefer relocating it into a container the design already has over adding a container the design does not.**

## ADR-073: Stayo Discover — one admin-gated visibility rule, its own public shell, and no invented listing data

- **Date:** 2026-08-15
- **Status:** accepted
- **Context:** `/` has forked into tenant and owner since [[Decisions#ADR-071|ADR-071]], but only the owner half led anywhere — the tenant panel rendered a disabled "Browse hostels" pill labelled *Coming soon*, because no hostel-browsing surface existed. The design (`Stayo Discover.dc.html`, Claude Design project `3f2fbde6`) covers twelve screens including a portable KYC profile, Aadhaar verification, star ratings and amenity grids. Auditing it against the schema showed the product is really four subsystems, of which only the first is buildable on what exists: **A** discovery + enquiry, **B** the portable profile (identity and documents currently hang off `tenants`, which is one row *per tenancy per hostel*, so nothing is portable today), **C** owner listing content (amenities, distances, bed tiers — unmodelled), **D** reviews and ratings (unmodelled). Phase A is what shipped.
- **Decision:**
  1. **One visibility predicate, exported and shared.** `DISCOVERABLE` in `src/services/discovery/discovery-service.ts` is the only definition of "appears in Discover" — `listing_status = LIVE` **and** `verification_status = VERIFIED` **and** `status = ACTIVE` **and** `admissions_enabled` **and** a non-null `public_slug` — and search and detail both use it. The failure it exists to prevent is specific: an admin suspends a hostel, it disappears from search, and its detail page stays reachable by direct URL forever because that route grew its own gate.
  2. **Discovery reads listing state and never writes it.** [[Decisions#ADR-040|ADR-040]] made `listing_status`/`verification_status` admin-controlled precisely so an owner cannot self-approve past platform verification. Discovery is a *consumer* of that decision; a test asserts the service exposes no write path to either column.
  3. **Detail composes `admissionsService.getPublicHostel()` rather than re-deriving vacancy** — that function already encodes capacity minus active allocations minus active room reservations minus active invitation reservations, and a second implementation is how two surfaces silently drift. Crucially it is **not** tightened to serve discovery: `/visit/:slug` is a direct-link admissions microsite with deliberately looser rules, so discovery layers its predicate *on top* and merges the two extra columns it needs (`hostel_type`, `food_included`) rather than widening a shared contract for one consumer.
  4. **An enquiry is a `visitor_leads` row, not a new entity.** Source `DISCOVER`, landing in the owner's existing inbox, scoring and funnel, and de-duplicated against the same `ACTIVE_LEAD_STATUSES` the microsite uses (now exported rather than copied). The owner must not acquire a second place to look. Approval routes into the pre-existing `POST /api/leads/[id]/convert-to-invitation`, so an accepted enquiry becomes a real tenant invitation feeding the onboarding flow — that seam is the point of the phase.
  5. **`/discover` is its own shell, outside `/tenant/*`.** Almost everyone using it has no tenancy; putting it behind `ProtectedTenantRoute` would force a sign-in before browsing (killing SEO and the top of the funnel) and would also bounce every seeker to `/complete-profile`, since that guard reads a field the tenant portal owns. There is **no route guard** on `/discover` at all — browse routes are public, and the authenticated tabs render an in-place sign-in prompt instead of redirecting, so tapping "Saved" does not lose the visitor's place. The server is the real boundary: every `/api/discover` route except browse requires a session.
  6. **The middleware allowlist entry is `/api/discover/hostels`, never `/api/discover`.** `PUBLIC_ROUTES` is prefix-matched, so the broader entry would have made every seeker's enquiry history and saved list world-readable.
  7. **No listing data is invented.** Star ratings, review counts, amenity grids and distance-to-campus are omitted rather than seeded or faked, and the API says so explicitly via `ratings_available: false` / `amenities_available: false`. An unpriced room renders "Price on request", never "₹0" — an owner who hasn't set a rent has not offered a free bed. This continues the rule ADR-072's amendment established for onboarding: **a surface must read real state and render nothing when it is absent, never a plausible fallback.**
  8. **No second signup path.** `authService.selfSignUpTenant()` already created exactly this account — `role = TENANT`, no `tenants` row, Supabase-linked at creation — and its own doc comment already called it "a marketplace account (browse/save/enquire)". Discovery builds the screen, not the endpoint. Its phone-OTP-over-WhatsApp gate is adopted as-is; an email-OTP alternative was designed and then discarded, because a parallel session-minting path is what [[Decisions#ADR-031|ADR-031]] exists to prevent.
- **Consequences:** listings look thinner than the prototype, and that is the intended trade — the gap is visible pressure to build phase C rather than a cosmetic problem to paper over. Vacancy and starting price are computed from room rows and cannot be expressed as SQL `WHERE`, so search fetches the matching set up to a 500-hostel cap and sorts/paginates in memory; if the listed-hostel count ever approaches that number the aggregate belongs in a materialised column, not a larger cap. `seeker_profile_id` is nullable forever — QR, walk-in and reception leads have no seeker account — so nothing may assume it is set. **Not verified against a live database:** the migration and both new tables need one real run; the 16 new tests use this repo's mocked-Prisma pattern.
- **Related:** [[Decisions#ADR-071|ADR-071]] (the fork this completes), [[Decisions#ADR-040|ADR-040]] (the boundary it consumes), [[Decisions#ADR-031|ADR-031]] (why signup was reused), [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Database]], [[Bugs]], [[Changelog]].

## ADR-074: The portable profile — identity beside `profiles`, a document vault shared per hostel, and owner edits demoted to proposals

- **Date:** 2026-08-15
- **Status:** accepted
- **Context:** phase A ([[Decisions#ADR-073|ADR-073]]) gave a person a durable Stayo account, and it was an empty shell. Every identity field — date of birth, guardian, college, permanent address — lives on `tenants`, which since migration 062 is **one row per tenancy per hostel**, and `identification_documents.tenant_id` ties each uploaded ID to a single tenancy. Moving to a second hostel therefore meant re-typing everything and re-uploading every document Stayo already held. The owner's stated requirement was blunter than "make it portable": a tenant must be able to complete their profile **before enquiring to any hostel at all**, with onboarding reading it as defaults. The profile is not a byproduct of onboarding; it is what onboarding reads.
- **Decision:**
  1. **`profile_identity`, a 1:1 table beside `profiles` — not columns on it.** `getSession()` reads a profile on every authenticated request for every role, and Prisma selects the full column set on any query without an explicit `select`. That is precisely the mechanism behind the 2026-08-14 outage, where a `tenants` column present in `schema.prisma` but absent from the database 500'd the entire authenticated API. Eighteen rarely-read columns on the hottest table in the system buys that risk for nothing.
  2. **`tenants` keeps its identity columns, as the tenancy-time snapshot.** Not deleted, not deprecated — they answer "what was true when this tenancy began", which is what history and agreements need. Reads are profile-first with a tenancy fallback, so this is a transition rather than a cutover and a half-migrated row still renders.
  3. **The vault separates the file from the verdict.** `identity_documents` is the person's file, uploaded once; `identity_document_shares` is one row per (document, hostel) carrying `PENDING`/`VERIFIED`/`REJECTED` and who decided. An owner sees a document only through a live share for a hostel they own. **Owner A's verification never becomes Owner B's** — the tenant re-uses the file, not the judgement. This resolves a contradiction in the design source, whose verify screen promises "shared only with the owner you enquire to" while its profile screen promises "verified once, reuse anywhere"; only one can be the default.
  4. **Person-level fields move from Category A to Category B.** Owner edits to `college_name`, `date_of_birth`, `gender`, `photo_url` and the rest become proposals the tenant approves, because under the old rules one owner would silently rewrite a person's record at a hostel they have nothing to do with. The test for Category B is not "is it sensitive" but **"does one hostel changing it affect another"**. Raised with the owner as a real cost to owner workflow, and approved before building.
  5. **The backfill is a script with a dry run, not an `UPDATE` in the migration.** A person with two past tenancies can hold two conflicting `college_name` values and neither is wrong. The script reports every conflict it resolved and every value it discarded; because §2 keeps the source columns, the losing values stay inspectable.
  6. **Agreements snapshot the identity they print.** `permanent_address`, `tenant_email` and `tenant_phone` were read **live** from the tenant row at PDF-render time. That was survivable while identity was per-tenancy and effectively frozen after activation; with a profile screen editing it, regenerating an old agreement would print an address the signatory never agreed to. Now written into `content_snapshot` at signing and read snapshot-first, with the `tenant.*` read kept as a fallback for agreements signed before the snapshot carried them.
- **Consequences:** the same field name now exists in two places for a while, which is deliberate and will rot if the transition never finishes — `pending_backfill_fields` is returned on every identity read specifically so the end of it is observable rather than guessed at. Owners lose immediate edit on academic and personal fields. Parent/guardian logins were considered and are **out of scope**: guardian remains fields on the tenant's own profile, not a second account with its own access. **Not verified against a live database** — migration 064, all three tables and the backfill need one real run; `DATABASE_URL_TEST` points at the same Supabase project as dev, so the accompanying 30 tests are fully mocked and were added to `vitest.pure.config.ts` rather than the DB-touching default config.
- **Related:** [[Decisions#ADR-073|ADR-073]] (the account this makes portable), [[Business-Rules]] (the three rules in full), [[Database]], [[APIs]], [[Features]], [[Changelog]].

## ADR-075: Residency history — disclosure is earned by engagement, and only facts travel

- **Date:** 2026-08-15
- **Status:** accepted (narrows [[Decisions#ADR-053|ADR-053]]; does not reverse it)
- **Context:** the owner asked for a tenant's stay history — which hostels, how long, which room, what rent — visible to the tenant themselves, and to owners when that person enquires or is being invited, framed as "where we make real use of the Stayo profile… building trust for owner as well as tenant". The history already existed as data: since [[Decisions#ADR-053|ADR-053]] a `tenants` row **is** one tenancy, and `room_allocations` + `move_out_requests` carry the rest. What it had never had was a reader — and ADR-053 explicitly forbade the owner-facing half, because a lookup that returns "lives at Sri Adithya, room 204, pays ₹6,500" for any email turns a cheap owner account into a way to find where a specific person lives.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Disclosure is earned by engagement, not granted by asking.** A hostel sees a person's history because that person **enquired to them** (an open `visitor_leads` row) or **holds a tenancy there**. An owner who merely types an identifier gets `allowed: false` and an empty list — never a count, never a hint that history exists. ADR-053's protection is intact; it now has an exception the tenant creates, not one the owner can take.
  2. **The tenant's "no" outranks engagement.** `residency_history_disclosures` stores `REQUESTED`/`APPROVED`/`DECLINED`/`REVOKED`, and a `REVOKED` or `DECLINED` row beats a tenancy that would otherwise disclose automatically. No row means "fall back to engagement". Access is otherwise *derived* rather than stored, because a derived rule cannot go stale or leak through a forgotten grant — when an enquiry closes, access ends by construction.
  3. **Only checkable facts travel.** Hostel, city, dates, duration, room number, sharing, rent, and whether the move-out settled. `exit_reason`, `exit_notes` and `tenant_behavior_scores` are **never** projected: they are one owner's unreviewed opinion, and letting them follow a person means a single bad exit blacklists them across every hostel on Stayo with no right of reply. A test asserts the projection cannot leak them.
  4. **An invitation the person never took up is not a stay.** Filtered on `activation_completed_at`, so an expired or cancelled invite does not appear as a tenancy someone abandoned.
  5. **Owners request; only tenants decide.** For the invite flow — where the person has not engaged and so has earned no disclosure — `POST /api/owner/tenant-history/request` creates a pending request. It grants nothing, and it deliberately **cannot re-open a `DECLINED`/`REVOKED` answer**, because a repeated ask is itself a message the tenant never consented to receiving.
  6. **Owner screens pass `tenant_id`, not `profile_id`.** Resolved server-side against hostels the caller owns, so no person-identifier travels through owner-facing UI state and an owner can only ever name their own tenancy.
- **Consequences:** the feature the owner asked for is delivered except at one point — history cannot appear while an owner *composes* an invite, only after the invitee engages or approves a request. That limit was stated before building and accepted. Rent becomes visible across owners, which is less sensitive than it was: Discover already publishes each hostel's starting price. The tenant's history screen doubles as the consent surface (who can see it, and a switch per hostel), because showing someone their record without showing them its readers erodes trust rather than building it. **Not verified against a live database** — migration 065 needs one real run; the 19 tests mock Prisma and live in `vitest.pure.config.ts`.
- **Related:** [[Decisions#ADR-053|ADR-053]] (the rule this narrows, with its amendment), [[Decisions#ADR-074|ADR-074]] (the same consent model, for documents), [[Business-Rules]], [[Database]], [[APIs]], [[Features]], [[Changelog]].

## ADR-076: The hostel marketing page is versioned content, and every version is approved before a tenant sees it

- **Date:** 2026-08-15
- **Status:** accepted (extends [[Decisions#ADR-040|ADR-040]] from the listing to its content)
- **Context:** Discovery ([[Decisions#ADR-073|ADR-073]]) shipped rendering only what the operational schema already knew — real rooms, real vacancy, real photos — and deliberately omitted amenities, distances and bed tiers because no column held them. The owner asked for the marketing page from `Stayo App.dc.html`'s `HOSTEL: MARKETING` section (lines 1647–1798, plus five edit modals), **with admin approval before anything is listed**. So this is two things at once: a content model, and a review cycle.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Revisions, not columns on `hostels`.** `hostel_marketing_revisions` holds one snapshot per version, with `DRAFT → PENDING_REVIEW → APPROVED` and the previously-approved revision becoming `SUPERSEDED`. The alternative — edit-in-place plus a "needs review" flag — fails on day one: an owner fixing a typo drops their hostel out of Discovery until an admin gets round to it, and a rejected edit takes down a page that was previously fine. With revisions the **APPROVED one keeps serving Discovery throughout**, and the admin approves a specific, diffable snapshot rather than "whatever the row says now". Partial unique indexes enforce one open and one approved revision per hostel. `SUPERSEDED` rows are kept, not deleted: they record what was advertised and when, which is what settles "the listing said ₹4,500".
  2. **The content is one validated JSONB column.** A revision is an immutable snapshot; child tables for photos/beds/places would each need their own versioning to belong to one, which is a second revision system to keep in step. `marketing-content.ts` validates the shape on the way in **and** on the way out — a revision approved under an older shape degrades to "no details yet" rather than 500ing a public page.
  3. **Two independent gates, and this is not the ADR-040 one.** `listing_status`/`verification_status` still decide whether a hostel is discoverable **at all**; an approved revision decides whether its *content* may be shown. A listing needs both, nothing in the marketing path writes those columns (asserted by test), and the admin queue says so on any hostel where the other gate is still shut — so an admin is never surprised that their approval didn't list anything.
  4. **The design's "Save & publish" becomes "Send to Stayo for review".** Nothing publishes on an owner's say-so; that is the whole point of the request. The design's status toggle becomes a real revision-state pill (Draft / In review / Live / Changes requested).
  5. **A submitted revision is locked.** An owner editing mid-review would mean the admin approves something other than what they read, so the editor locks and offers *withdraw* instead. Rejections **require** a note, because it is the owner's only route forward and a reasonless "no" just produces a resubmission of the same listing.
  6. **The reviewer is given what they cannot see by eye:** advertised price against real `min(rooms.base_rent)` (5% tolerance, so it does not cry wolf), and bed tiers claiming sharing types the hostel has no rooms for. **Neither blocks approval** — an owner may legitimately run an introductory rate — they are surfaced with both numbers for a human to judge. This is the concrete answer to "what is approval *for*".
  7. **Owner-authored price is what Discovery advertises; live vacancy still comes from real rooms.** A marketing tier says what is on sale, not what is free. A tier the owner marks `FULL` is full regardless; otherwise the live count wins over any claim.
  8. **Reviews have no owner-writable field at all.** The design states "Managed by Stayo — owners can't add, edit or remove reviews", and that is a trust property rather than a layout choice, so the content schema simply has nowhere to put one. A test pins it.
- **Consequences:** every listing change now costs a human review, which is the intended trade and also a real operational load — the queue is at `/admin/marketing-reviews` and nothing lands in Discovery without passing through it. **Template reuse across hostels is deferred**: the design's "Switch / reuse" and "Save as new" render disabled, because one page serving several hostels means one edit re-opens review for all of them and the reviewer needs to see which hostels a change affects. Photos are added by URL for now — ImageKit upload exists elsewhere in the app and wiring it in is its own work; a URL field stores exactly what will render rather than pretending an upload happened. **Not verified against a live database** — migration 066 needs one real run.
- **Related:** [[Decisions#ADR-040|ADR-040]] (the boundary this extends), [[Decisions#ADR-073|ADR-073]] (the surface it fills in), [[Business-Rules]], [[Database]], [[APIs]], [[Features]], [[Changelog]].

## ADR-077: The listing's mess menu is reviewed marketing content, not the operational food schedule

- **Date:** 2026-08-15
- **Status:** accepted (extends [[Decisions#ADR-076|ADR-076]] to a section it did not cover)
- **Context:** Rebuilding the owner marketing page against its design (`Stayo App.dc.html`) surfaced a "Mess menu" card the previous implementation had dropped entirely, and the Discovery listing design (`Stayo Discover.dc.html`) has a matching "Food & mess" section — a weekly menu of 4 meals × 7 days that a tenant reads *before* deciding to move in. Stayo already stores a weekly menu: `food_schedules` + `food_schedule_meals` (7 days × 4 meal types, one schedule per hostel per month, DRAFT/PUBLISHED — see [[Food]]). The obvious move was to point the listing at it. `MarketingContent` had no mess field at all, so something had to be decided either way.
- **Decision:** the listing carries **its own reviewed copy** of the menu, as a `mess` block inside `hostel_marketing_revisions.content`. Discovery does **not** read `food_schedules`.
  1. **A food schedule is a tenant-ops artifact; a listing is a reviewed claim.** The schedule is regenerated monthly, can be driven by resident polls (ADR-057), and changes whenever an owner reworks next month's plan. Wiring the public listing to it would let the menu a prospective tenant was shown change with no review at all — which is precisely the property [[Decisions#ADR-076|ADR-076]] exists to establish for every other part of the listing.
  2. **It would also tie a marketing promise to a month that may not exist.** A hostel with no schedule for next month is a normal state; a listing that silently loses its "Food & mess" section on the 1st is not.
  3. **No migration.** `content` is already a JSON column validated by a zod schema, so this is additive: `MessSchema` in `marketing-content.ts`. Old revisions parse to "no mess provided".
  4. **The four meals are a fixed set.** Owners edit dishes and can switch a meal off, but cannot invent a fifth — a listing where each hostel names its own meals stops being comparable in search. `normaliseContent` restores the set (preserving each meal's own saved edits) and pads `week` to exactly 7 rows, because both surfaces index it positionally and Tuesday must never read `undefined`.
  5. **`provided` defaults to false.** An unstated claim is false: silence must not render as "meals included".
  6. **The reviewer sees the whole week.** The admin review screen gained a Mess block — approving a menu you cannot read is not approval.
- **Consequences:** the same menu now lives in two places for a hostel that runs both, and they can legitimately disagree — the listing shows what was approved, the Food tab shows what is actually being cooked this month. That is the intended split, not drift, but it means an owner who changes the real menu has to also update the listing and wait for review. **If that friction proves wrong in practice**, the fix is to let an owner *import* the current schedule into the listing draft as a starting point — still passing through review — rather than to make Discovery read the schedule live. **Not verified against a live database**: the mess round-trip is covered by unit tests over `normaliseContent` and the discovery projection, not by a real owner saving a menu end to end.
- **Related:** [[Decisions#ADR-076|ADR-076]] (the cycle this joins), [[Decisions#ADR-057|ADR-057]] (the food polls feeding the *other* menu), [[Food]], [[Business-Rules]], [[APIs]], [[Features]], [[Changelog]].

## ADR-078: Explore / Dashboard / Profile — a shared app-wide nav, one common profile, and Google-only tenant signup

- **Date:** 2026-08-16
- **Status:** accepted (point 2 explicitly **supersedes** [[Decisions#ADR-068|ADR-068]]'s "no Complaints tab" call)
- **Context:** the tenant-facing app had two unrelated navigation systems that grew independently — the Tenant Dashboard (`TenantAppShell`, 5 tabs, ADR-068) and Stayo Discover (`DiscoverShell`, 4 tabs, ADR-073), each with its own bottom bar, and Discover's portable profile (ADR-074) sitting beside, not replacing, the Dashboard's own tenancy-scoped profile tab. Explicit product direction: collapse this into one mental model — **Explore** (find a hostel), **Dashboard** (manage a stay you have), **Profile** (your one identity) — with the bottom nav depending on tenancy state, and simplify tenant signup to Google-only with phone verification moved to the moment it's actually needed (sending an enquiry).
- **Decision:**
  1. **One shared outer nav, state-dependent.** New `app/nav/{appNavConfig.ts,useAppNav.ts}` is the single source of truth for which tabs show: **Explore | Profile** (no live tenancy) or **Explore | Dashboard | Profile** (live tenancy — `tenant_status` `INVITED`/`ACTIVE`, the same definition `profile-identity-service.ts` already used, reused rather than redefined). New `AppShell`/`AppBottomNav` (`app/layouts/AppShell.tsx`, `app/components/AppBottomNav.tsx`) render it; `DiscoverShell`'s nav role is retired (its non-nav helpers stay); `TenantAppShell` is repurposed from an outer fixed-bottom bar into the Dashboard's own inner `sticky top-0` strip, so the two nav layers never stack. `tenant_status` is threaded through `/auth/me` and every login path (`login`, `loginWithPhone`, tenant-activation auto-login) via `createSessionAndTokens()` — the frontend previously discarded a field the backend already computed.
  2. **Dashboard's internal tabs become Home/Money/Room/Food/Complaints, superseding ADR-068's 5-tab design (Home/Money/Room/Food/**Profile**).** Complaints is a new tab (`TenantComplaintsPage`, ticket UI carried over from the dissolved `TenantProfilePage`) under new explicit product direction — not a design-fidelity finding this time, since no `.dc.html` source depicts a 6th tab. Profile moves out of the Dashboard entirely, promoted to the shared outer tab.
  3. **One common Stayo Profile**, not per-hostel. The existing portable-profile hub (`DiscoverProfilePage.tsx`, `profile_identity`, ADR-074) is promoted from Discover-only to app-wide (`/profile/*`, new `ProfileRoutes.tsx`), absorbing `TenantProfilePage`'s tenancy-scoped Personal/Contact/Emergency/Academic groups (kept on `useTenantProfile()`, **not** merged into `profile_identity` — the governed-phone/email owner-approval routing (ADR-069) stays exactly as built, reusing `ProfileEditScreen`/`buildProfileEditConfigs` rather than rewriting that logic) and Documents (shown as two sections — portable vault + per-tenancy KYC — not unified, since unifying two genuinely different data models is separate backend work). Saved/Enquiries move here too (`/profile/saved`, `/profile/enquiries`), off the primary nav.
  4. **One canonical profile-completion percentage**, not three. `profile-identity-service.ts`'s `getIdentity()` gains `completion_percent` (profile-type-aware denominator — core + general-optional + whichever of academic/professional applies, not a naive 20-field count that would unfairly cap a working professional). Both the Explore/Profile nudge and a new Dashboard `ProfileCompletionNudge` (on `TenantHomePage`) read the same field via the same query — replacing three previously-independent ad hoc computations (old `TenantProfilePage`'s, the frozen portal page's, Discover's boolean-only version).
  5. **Dashboard entry is no longer gated on profile completeness.** `ProtectedTenantRoute` drops its `is_profile_completed` hard redirect to the legacy `/complete-profile` wizard (a nudge card replaces it) and *gains* a tenancy-liveness check it was missing — closing a real gap where a Discover-only seeker with `is_profile_completed` happening to be `true` could already reach `/tenant/*` with nothing stopping them. `is_profile_completed` still exists and still gates the separate invited-tenant onboarding wizard.
  6. **Owner-invited tenant activation is unchanged** — `ActivationPage.tsx` already lands a freshly-activated tenant directly at `/tenant/home`, correctly skipping Explore; this decision doesn't touch that flow.
  7. **Tenant signup becomes Google-only, via a new, narrow auto-provisioning path — [[Decisions#ADR-031|ADR-031]]'s single-session-authority principle is not weakened.** The existing password/phone-OTP signup form is removed from `LoginModal`'s Sign Up tab (replaced with a single "Continue with Google" action); `AuthContext.signUpTenant()`/`POST /api/auth/tenant-signup` are left in place, just unwired from this UI. `resolveSupabaseSession()` (`lib/auth/supabase-session.ts`) — hard-tested since ADR-031 to never auto-provision — is **not modified**. A new, separate function, `provisionMarketplaceTenantFromSupabase()` (`lib/auth/supabase-provision.ts`), reachable only from a new `POST /api/auth/google/provision` endpoint (itself requiring an already-valid Supabase session), creates a `profiles` row **only** when none exists for that email at all, then delegates back to the untouched `resolveSupabaseSession()` for payload-building — one implementation, not two. It never mints a session itself; Supabase remains the sole session-minting authority. Rate-limited via the existing `checkFixedWindowLimit` primitive. `tests/auth-hardening-security.test.ts` passes unmodified; a new `tests/auth-google-provisioning.test.ts` covers the new path in the same static-analysis style.
  8. **Phone verification moves from signup-time to enquiry-time, and asks at most once.** `EnquiryPage` now gates submission on `user.phone_verified` (new field on `/auth/me`/`AuthUser`) instead of just `isSeeker`. No database schema change was needed — `profile.phone`/`phone_verified` and the OTP-verification table already existed for the old signup flow; this reuses them via the existing `authApi.sendPhoneOtp`/`verifyPhoneOtp` calls and one small correctness fix to the pre-existing (and previously unused-by-any-frontend-code) `PATCH /api/profile` route — changing `phone` now correctly re-arms `phone_verified: false` when the number actually changes, and returns a friendly `409 PHONE_TAKEN` instead of a raw 500.
- **Consequences:** two "personal email" fields can legitimately coexist on the merged Profile page for a live-tenancy user (`profile_identity.personal_email`, portable and free; `tenants.personal_email`, this hostel's governed copy) — flagged as a known follow-up if it proves confusing, not resolved here. Room's own service-request tiles still duplicate Complaints' ticket-raising UI (both write to the same `tenant_service_requests` table) — accepted, not consolidated, to keep this change's footprint bounded. `docs/obsidian/Features.md`'s Tenant-facing and Public-facing sections, and this file's own ADR-068 entry, are updated in the same change per the standing documentation rule.
- **Related:** [[Decisions#ADR-068|ADR-068]] (superseded), [[Decisions#ADR-069|ADR-069]] (governed fields, unchanged), [[Decisions#ADR-073|ADR-073]] (Discover, its shell retired), [[Decisions#ADR-074|ADR-074]] (portable profile, promoted app-wide), [[Decisions#ADR-031|ADR-031]] (single session authority, upheld), [[Features]], [[Business-Rules]], [[APIs]], [[Changelog]].

## ADR-079: Profile → Raise a Ticket is a second, independent ticket system — Stayo Admin-bound, never owner-bound

- **Date:** 2026-08-16
- **Status:** accepted
- **Context:** [[Decisions#ADR-078|ADR-078]] gave Dashboard a Complaints tab (`/tenant/complaints`, `tenant_service_requests`) — but that system is, and was always meant to be, tenant → **owner/hostel**: every row carries `owner_id`/`hostel_id`, and its only recipients are that hostel's staff. There was no route anywhere for a signed-in Stayo account to report a problem with the **Stayo app or website itself** to Stayo. An initial proposal to fold "Report an issue" into the common Profile by linking it at `/tenant/complaints` was rejected: that would have quietly repurposed an owner-facing table as a Stayo-Admin-facing one, collapsing two audiences (a hostel's staff; Stayo's own support team) that must never read each other's queue.
- **Decision:**
  1. **A new, independent table — `platform_support_tickets`.** Neither existing candidate fit: `tenant_service_requests` and the unused `complaints` table are both `owner_id`/`hostel_id`-bound by design. The new table has **no `owner_id`, no `hostel_id`, and no relation to `tenants`** — modeled directly on `owner_documents`' shape (`profile_id`-scoped, `status` only an admin can advance, `resolved_at`/`resolved_by`/`admin_note`), the existing platform-admin review-queue pattern, rather than inventing a new one.
  2. **`/tenant/complaints` stays exactly what it was** — the tenant → owner channel, reachable from Room (and, per this decision, linkable from Food/Payments later if needed) — untouched by this change, no shared code, no shared table.
  3. **Available to any signed-in Stayo account, not gated on tenancy.** The report is about Stayo, not a hostel stay, so requiring a live tenancy would exclude exactly the accounts (a seeker who hit a bug before ever renting) most likely to need it. Lives in the common Profile as its **own standalone section** ("Raise a ticket"), explicitly not inside Support & settings — a deliberate signal that its destination (Stayo Admin) differs from everything else on that page (which either does nothing yet or opens a `mailto:`).
  4. **Admin review reuses the platform-admin pattern wholesale, not a new one.** `GET /api/platform-admin/support-tickets?status=` + `POST .../[id]/resolve`, gated `session.role === "ADMIN"`, mirrors `owner-documents`' route pair field-for-field. `AdminSupportTicketsPage` mirrors `AdminDocumentsPage`'s list/detail queue shape, simplified (flat list, no file viewer — a ticket is text, not an upload).
  5. **No 7th primary admin tab.** `ADMIN_TABS` (`AdminAppShell.tsx`) is pixel-matched to `Stayo Admin.dc.html`'s fixed 6-item sidebar; `/admin/marketing-reviews` already established that a real review queue can be reached without a primary slot. This one is reached via a new row on `/admin/more`, next to (not replacing) the existing `mailto:` "Support" row — that row is the admin's own way to reach Stayo's broader team, a different purpose from reviewing tickets *reported to* Stayo.
- **Consequences:** two systems now exist for "something is wrong," and a user has to pick the right one at the point of reporting (Room/Food/Payments issue → owner; app/website issue → Stayo). The distinction is stated in-product (Profile's row says "Report a Stayo app or website problem") rather than solved by unifying them, since unifying would mean either exposing hostel complaints to Stayo Admin or exposing app-bug reports to individual hostel owners — neither of which is correct. Food and Payments do not yet get dedicated contextual complaint entry points (both still route through the existing `/tenant/complaints` hub) — deferred, not forgotten, per explicit scope-narrowing. **Not verified against a live database via automated tests** — the migration was applied and the Prisma client regenerated against the real dev database, and both new route families were smoke-tested against the running dev server (401 unauthenticated, not 500), but the backend vitest suite could not run in this environment (`DATABASE_URL_TEST`/`.env.test` absent — a pre-existing local-environment gap unrelated to this change).
- **Related:** [[Decisions#ADR-078|ADR-078]] (the Complaints tab this stays independent from), [[Decisions#ADR-038|ADR-038]] (`owner_documents`, the pattern this reuses), [[Database]], [[APIs]], [[Business-Rules]], [[Features]], [[Changelog]].

## ADR-080: The admin console is replaced wholesale, and unbuilt screens must look unbuilt

- **Date:** 2026-08-16
- **Status:** accepted (supersedes the console shape set by [[Decisions#ADR-030|ADR-030]]; amends [[Decisions#ADR-076|ADR-076]]'s queue location)
- **Context:** A new `Stayo Admin.dc.html` arrived with ten sections, a six-variant detail drawer and a toast system — a substantially larger console than the 2026-07-26 one (`AdminAppShell` + 9 pages). The owner's direction was explicit: remove the existing admin dashboard entirely and replace it with this design, wired to the real Stayo workflow. Auditing the design against the backend found the split that shapes everything below: Owners, Listings, KYC, Revenue, Subscriptions and Broadcasts have real endpoints; **Settlements' routes all return 410 Gone** (decommissioned, no Prisma tables) and **Reports & Bugs has no model at all**, and the owner confirmed both backends are to be designed later.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Replace, don't migrate.** All 9 legacy pages and `AdminAppShell` are deleted rather than restyled. The design changes the information architecture (owner-first, four nav groups, a drawer instead of detail pages), so incremental restyling would have meant maintaining two IAs at once.
  2. **Unbuilt screens render `NotWiredYet`, never a plausible zero.** One component, one wording, used for Settlements, Reports & Bugs, the revenue calendar and KYC's business-details panel. A zeroed stat or a sample row would let an admin read an unbuilt feature as a real one reporting that business is quiet — a worse failure than showing nothing, because it is silent. `EmptyState` is deliberately a *different* component: "nothing to review" and "this isn't built" must not look alike.
  3. **Keep tested pure logic that survived the deletion.** `leadQueue.ts`, `ownerHealth.ts`, `documentQueue.ts`, `revenueFormat.ts`, `needsAttention.ts` carry test-covered business rules with no UI in them. Deleting them to re-derive them worse was the one clearly wasteful move available.
  4. **Settings stays, though the design omits it.** It is the only home for admin invites, notification templates and support-contact editing; dropping it to match the design exactly would have removed working functionality with no replacement. It is added to the Support group.
  5. **Marketing review becomes a tab inside Hostel Listings**, not a sidebar entry. [[Decisions#ADR-076|ADR-076]]'s two-gate distinction is preserved and is arguably clearer beside the other gate; `/admin/marketing-reviews` redirects to `/admin/listings?tab=content`.
  6. **The drawer's identity lives in the URL** (`?detail=<kind>:<id>`), so a reviewer can link a colleague to a specific submission and a refresh mid-review doesn't lose their place. An unknown kind resolves to *closed*, not to an empty drawer that reads as a stuck spinner.
  7. **KYC groups by owner, not by document.** The endpoint returns one row per file, but the decision is about a person; grouping is also what keeps the sidebar badge and the screen agreeing on the size of the backlog.
- **Consequences:** the console is smaller in capability than it looks — six of eleven screens are layout-only at first ship, which is honest but does mean the sidebar advertises destinations that cannot yet do work. Two regressions were caught and fixed during the rebuild rather than shipped: the console's **only sign-out control** lived on the deleted More page (now in the sidebar footer, covered by `logoutIntegrity.test.ts`), and the old console had been rendering **Playfair Display where the design specifies Manrope**, because it reused the shared `.font-display` token. **Not verified against a live database or a real admin session** — every screen is wired to real endpoints and typechecks, but no end-to-end run against live data has happened.
- **Related:** [[Decisions#ADR-030|ADR-030]] (why the admin persona exists), [[Decisions#ADR-076|ADR-076]] (the queue this relocates), [[Frontend]], [[Features]], [[Changelog]].

## ADR-081: The sales funnel and the activation funnel are one lifecycle, with split ownership

- **Date:** 2026-08-16
- **Status:** accepted (extends [[Decisions#ADR-032|ADR-032]]'s approval flow; implemented by migration 067)
- **Context:** The admin design ships a *sales* pipeline (New → Contacted → Demo → Negotiating → Converted). The codebase implements an *activation* funnel (`NEW → APPROVED → INVITE_SENT → OWNER_ACTIVATED → HOSTEL_CREATED → LIVE`) whose later stages fire real side effects — approving mints a single-use token and messages the owner. Building the design literally would have meant a second status column, or a UI where "Demo" silently emailed somebody. The owner's direction settled it: these are the front and back halves of one journey — landing page → contact → negotiation → invite → activation → 14-day trial → subscription.
- **Decision:** one enum, ownership split by stage.
  1. **`PlatformLeadStatus` gains `CONTACTED`, `DEMO`, `NEGOTIATING`.** One column keeps one answer to "where is this lead"; two columns could disagree, and something would have had to arbitrate.
  2. **The split is enforced where it already was.** `MANUALLY_SETTABLE_STATUSES` in `leads/[id]/route.ts` was already the thing guaranteeing "no manual status writes after an invite is sent" — this widens that array rather than inventing a mechanism. `APPROVABLE_STATUSES` and `canRejectLead` widen to match, so a lead can be approved or declined straight out of a negotiation.
  3. **Advancing stops at NEGOTIATING.** The next step is approval, which has side effects, so it sits behind its own button. "Next stage" must never be the control that emails a customer.
  4. **The new stages are internal and must never reach the applicant.** `lead-stage-mapper.ts` feeds the public `/enquiry/:token` page; all three collapse into "Under review". This is the highest-consequence detail in the change — a prospect reading "Negotiating" about themselves learns they are being haggled with — so it is pinned by tests that assert the words appear nowhere in the rendered timeline.
  5. **`UNDER_REVIEW` rows migrate to `CONTACTED`; the enum value stays.** Postgres cannot drop an enum member without recreating the type, and leaving it costs nothing once nothing writes it.
  6. **Lost reasons are a structured enum; discovery answers are not.** `pain_point`/`current_tooling` are deliberately free-form (their own schema comment warns a reworded option produces a new distinct value). So the insights tab *counts* `lost_reason` and *lists* discovery as quotes. Charting free text would manufacture categories out of phrasing and typos.
  7. **Outreach logging never changes the stage.** Three "no answer" entries are not progress, and auto-advancing on the first connected call would take the stage decision away from the admin who made the call.
  8. **A dedicated `platform_lead_activities` table, not `systemEventLog`.** That table is the automated audit trail and carries `lead_id` inside `metadata` with no column (a reuse tradeoff recorded earlier). The outreach log is human-authored, written during a call, and read on every drawer open — it needs its own index. The drawer merges both feeds.
- **Consequences:** the funnel is now genuinely reportable, and the Overview's acquisition funnel gains real middle stages. The cost is that lead status is now edited by hand far more often, so the manual/system boundary matters more than it did — if a future route writes status directly it can bypass `MANUALLY_SETTABLE_STATUSES`, which is worth a coverage check if it recurs. **Not verified against a live database**: migration 067 is written and the Prisma client generates, but it has not been applied — the repo applies SQL by hand via Supabase (see `migrations/README.md`), so the new columns and tables are unexercised against real rows.
- **Related:** [[Decisions#ADR-032|ADR-032]], [[Decisions#ADR-080|ADR-080]] (the console this lives in), [[Database]], [[APIs]], [[Features]], [[Changelog]].

## ADR-082: Stayo lists hostels it does not manage, and hands them over when the owner joins

- **Date:** 2026-08-16
- **Status:** accepted (extends [[Decisions#ADR-076|ADR-076]] to admin-authored content; implemented by migration 068)
- **Context:** Discovery could only ever show hostels whose owners had signed up, which makes it useless as a search surface in any city Stayo has not yet sold into. The owner asked for two things: finish the marketing review loop (show the admin everything submitted, preview it, flag specific parts), and let admins author listings for hostels with no Stayo owner — assignable to that owner if they later join.
- **Decision:**
  1. **`hostels.listing_source` + a sentinel owner, not a nullable `owner_id`.** A platform listing points at an inactive "Stayo Platform" profile until claimed. Nullable ownership would force every owner-scoped query in the codebase to handle a hostel with nobody, and `architectural-invariants-check.ts` already forbids treating hostel ownership as optional. The sentinel is `is_active: false` with no credentials — a foreign-key placeholder, not an account.
  2. **An absent `listing_source` reads as OWNER_MANAGED.** Every hostel predating migration 068 has no value there; defaulting the other way would make the entire existing estate claimable — one API call from handing a live hostel with real tenants to a stranger. Pinned by test.
  3. **Claiming refuses on any owner-managed hostel.** Transferring a live hostel moves its tenants, obligations and payouts; that is a different and far more dangerous operation and must not share this code path.
  4. **Claiming never touches `listing_status`/`verification_status`.** Deciding *who owns* a listing is not deciding *whether it is discoverable*; conflating them would let a claim silently publish or unpublish.
  5. **A platform listing never shows live vacancy.** It has no real `rooms` — nobody operates it here — so its bed tiers are an advertised claim. `projectListing` returns `availability_confirmed: false` and the UI must say so. Advertising beds nobody can honour, to someone trying to find somewhere to live, is the worst failure available in this feature.
  6. **One projection, shared by preview and the live page.** `listing-projection.ts` was extracted from `getListing` so the admin previews through the exact code tenants see. A second renderer drifts, and once it does the review gate inspects something other than what ships.
  7. **Per-section review flags.** Send-back carries `[{section, note}]`; either a flag or a note suffices (a flagged section *is* an instruction), neither is refused — enforced once, in `isSendBackActionable`. The owner's notification names the sections.
  8. **Enquiries on unclaimed listings raise a `platform_leads` row**, named after the hostel rather than the enquiring tenant, with an empty phone: a listing's number belongs to the business, not to someone who consented to contact.
- **Consequences:** Discovery can cover a city ahead of sales, and demand becomes the pitch — but Stayo now publishes pages for businesses that never agreed to be listed, which is a real editorial and legal posture, not just a feature. Admin-authored listings are approved by their author (no second reviewer); recorded in the audit trail and revisitable once the scoped-access work lands a `LISTINGS`-scoped reviewer. **Not verified against a live database or in a browser** — migrations 067 and 068 are unapplied outside test, and no flow here has been exercised end to end.
- **Related:** [[Decisions#ADR-076|ADR-076]], [[Decisions#ADR-080|ADR-080]], [[Decisions#ADR-081|ADR-081]], [[Database]], [[APIs]], [[Features]], [[Changelog]].

## ADR-083: Meal Timings live in `preferences_config`, not a new table — permanent config, separate from the changing weekly menu

- **Date:** 2026-08-19
- **Status:** accepted
- **Context:** `food_schedule_meals` has always stored a day-of-week × meal-type dish name and nothing else — no meal has ever had a time of day anywhere in the schema. The only place a "meal time" existed at all was a hardcoded frontend constant, `owner-food/weekGrid.ts`'s `MEAL_TIMES` (a single hour per meal — 8am/1pm/5pm/8pm), used only to pick "current/next meal" for the owner's Today card. It was never editable, never shown to a tenant, and not real per-hostel data. `docs/obsidian/Food.md` §15 flagged this as an open gap; `weekGrid.ts` itself already carried a doc comment anticipating the fix: *"If these need to become per-hostel, `preferences_config` already exists for exactly this kind of setting; no new table."* The request was to make serving windows owner-configurable (start/end per meal, enable/disable), reused everywhere a meal is shown — the weekly menu, the tenant Food page, tenant Home — without ever being re-entered.
- **Decision:**
  1. **A new `meal_timings` key inside `hostels.preferences_config` (JSON), not a new table.** Shape: `{ BREAKFAST: {start, end, enabled}, LUNCH: {...}, SNACKS: {...}, DINNER: {...} }`, `start`/`end` as `"HH:mm"` 24h strings. One row per hostel, four fixed keys — proportionate for the same reason billing defaults, receipts and branding already live in this column rather than their own tables. Zero migration required.
  2. **This deliberately avoids the exact risk `docs/obsidian/Food.md` §16 already documents**: a hand-written Food migration (`food_polls`) was applied to dev but missed production for 10 days, causing a live 500. A JSON-key addition ships with no migration at all.
  3. **Pure normalize/validate functions, not a throwing service class.** `apps/backend/lib/services/food/meal-timings.ts` — `normalizeMealTimings(rawConfig)` (read path, per-meal fallback to defaults so one corrupted key never blanks the other three) and `sanitizeMealTimingsPayload(payload)` (write path, throws `VALIDATION: ...` matching `hostel-billing-preferences-service.ts`'s convention). No Prisma import, so it's testable under `npm run test:pure` without a database — this environment has no `DATABASE_URL_TEST` configured, and `test:pure`'s existing allowlist config is exactly the workaround already established for this gap (see its own doc comment, and the sibling `food-meal-swap.test.ts`/`food-schedule-rebuild-policy.test.ts` pattern this follows).
  4. **Validation is same-meal-only.** `start < end` is enforced per meal (no meal may span midnight in v1); overlap *between different* meal types is deliberately not rejected — a hostel may legitimately run Lunch and Snacks close together.
  5. **Never blank for an unconfigured hostel.** Every read (owner screen, weekly-grid header, both tenant surfaces) falls back to `DEFAULT_MEAL_TIMINGS` (07:00–09:00 / 12:30–14:00 / 17:00–18:00 / 19:00–21:00) rather than 404ing or rendering nothing — every hostel that existed before this shipped gets sensible defaults with no backfill needed.
  6. **Status/countdown logic is a frontend pure module, not server-computed.** `apps/frontend/src/features/food/mealTimings.ts` (`mealStatusAt`, `nextServingAt`, `formatCountdown`, `formatTimeRange`) ticks against a local `useNow()` interval, not a refetch — a live "Starts in 42 min" has to update every 30s regardless of whether the underlying config changed, and computing it server-side would mean either polling or clock-skew risk for no benefit.
  7. **One "next serving" lookup, composed by each surface, not duplicated.** The owner's Today card (`currentAndNextMeal`, always resolves to a slot, skips disabled meals) and the tenant's Next Serving card (`nextServingAt`, returns null once the day's meals are done) are two different questions with two different answers for the same underlying data — deliberately two functions rather than one overloaded contract.
- **Consequences:** the weekly schedule grid gained a read-only meal-time legend (not a per-cell field) — satisfying "never re-entered" structurally, since there is no time field on `food_schedule_meals` to re-enter into. Per-day-different timings (a named future extension) can be added later as a sibling JSON key with lookups falling back to the base set — no shape change to what shipped now. **Verified**: `npm run test:pure` (backend, 18 new tests) and the full frontend suite (both `mealTimings.test.ts` and the updated `weekGrid.test.ts`) pass; both new routes smoke-tested against the running dev server (401 unauthenticated, not 500); production build succeeds. Not verified against a live database with real auth — no `DATABASE_URL_TEST` in this environment (pre-existing gap, unrelated to this change).
- **Related:** [[Decisions#ADR-077|ADR-077]] (the other Food-module storage-location precedent for `preferences_config`-style JSON), [[Database]], [[APIs]], [[Business-Rules]], [[Features]], [[Food]], [[Changelog]].

## ADR-084: A shared hostel link is a server-rendered preview page, not the SPA URL

- **Date:** 2026-08-19
- **Status:** accepted
- **Context:** Seekers had no way to send a hostel to a friend and owners no way to send their own listing anywhere. Copying the address bar does not solve it: `apps/frontend` is a **static Vite SPA**, so every path serves one `index.html` carrying site-wide Open Graph tags, and a listing URL pasted into WhatsApp unfurls as the generic "Stayo | Hostel Management Platform" card. Link crawlers (WhatsApp, facebookexternalhit, TelegramBot, Slackbot) do not execute JavaScript, so no client-side meta-tag library can change what they read.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Share `yourstayo.com/h/:slug`, not `/discover/h/:slug`.** A `vercel.json` rewrite sends it to `GET /api/discover/share/[slug]` on the backend, which returns HTML with per-hostel OG tags — the same arrangement `/pay/:token` already uses, so this is an established route in this repo rather than a new deployment concept. The rewrite must precede the SPA catch-all, which otherwise swallows it.
  2. **Rejected: user-agent sniffing on the canonical URL.** Rewriting `/discover/h/:slug` only for known crawler UAs keeps one URL, but any chat app not on the list silently falls back to the generic card, and the list is a maintenance burden that fails silently when it rots. A dedicated path works for every crawler with no guessing.
  3. **Same visibility predicate, no second copy.** The route reads `discoveryService.getShareCard`, gated by the one `DISCOVERABLE` constant. A hostel an admin suspends must stop previewing in every chat it was ever pasted into; a private copy of that rule is exactly the drift the constant exists to prevent.
