  4. **An unlisted slug renders a page with no hostel identity** — no name, no photo, the site cover only, `noindex`, HTTP 404, `Cache-Control: no-store`. A de-listed hostel must not keep unfurling its own photograph in fifty existing chats.
  5. **The description states only facts we hold** — starting price (or "Price on request", never ₹0), sharing sizes, meals, verified. This is the most-read sentence Stayo writes about a hostel; one unbackable claim there devalues every claim on the listing.
  6. **`navigator.share` first, clipboard as the fallback.** The OS sheet is the only route to Instagram and the only list that knows which apps the person has. Dismissing it (`AbortError`) is a no-op, not a failure — copying a link at someone who just cancelled makes cancel feel broken.
  7. **No share buttons on result cards, and no photo-as-file attachment.** The card already carries a heart, badges and a beds-free pill; and file sharing is unsupported on desktop and patchy in Android WebViews, while the OG card already delivers the photo.
- **Consequences:** Every hostel now has a public, crawler-facing URL that renders without a session — deliberately, since a stranger's WhatsApp must be able to fetch it. It exposes nothing the public listing does not. `middleware.ts`'s `PUBLIC_ROUTES` gains `/api/discover/share` (prefix-matched, and deliberately narrower than `/api/discover`). Two URLs now address one hostel, resolved with `rel="canonical"` pointing at the listing. **Unverified in production:** that `api.yourstayo.com` serves this path exactly as it serves `/api/payments/pay/` is proven only by the first deploy.
- **Related:** [[Decisions#ADR-073|ADR-073]], [[Decisions#ADR-076|ADR-076]], [[APIs]], [[Frontend]], [[Features]], [[Changelog]].

## ADR-085: The listing's mess menu is a copy of the kitchen's, taken on request

- **Date:** 2026-08-19
- **Status:** accepted (fills the gap [[Decisions#ADR-077|ADR-077]] left open)
- **Context:** ADR-077 keeps the listing's mess menu as its own reviewed copy rather than reading `food_schedules` live, and that reasoning holds — a food schedule is regenerated monthly and can be driven by a residents' poll, so wiring the listing to it would let the menu a tenant is shown *before* moving in change with no review at all. What it left unsolved is that an owner already maintaining a weekly menu in Food had to retype all 28 cells into the listing by hand. Most did not: they reported being unable to put their menu on the marketing page at all.
- **Decision:** A **one-time copy, on the owner's request.** `GET /api/owner/hostels/[id]/marketing/kitchen-menu` returns the current month's PUBLISHED schedule (falling back to the most recent published one — on the 1st, before the new month is generated, last month's is what the kitchen is still cooking) mapped into the listing's 7×4 grid. The mess editor's "Copy this hostel's kitchen menu" button fills the **draft**, which the owner then edits and sends through the ordinary review cycle. Nothing is saved by the import itself, and no live link is created.
- **Consequences:** The listing's menu can drift from the kitchen's, and that is intentional — the published one is what Stayo reviewed. The mapping skips any day or meal it does not recognise rather than guessing, and always returns exactly seven days, because both surfaces index the week positionally.
- **Related:** [[Decisions#ADR-077|ADR-077]], [[Food]], [[Features]], [[APIs]].

## ADR-086: Reviews are written by signed-in accounts and published only by an admin

- **Date:** 2026-08-19
- **Status:** accepted
- **Context:** Discovery has returned `ratings_available: false` since launch, reserving the space for reviews rather than inventing a number. Listings therefore had no social proof at all, and nothing explained the absence — a blank where reviews belong reads as neglect. The ask was a review box on the listing, with **control of what appears held by admins**.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Signed-in accounts only.** An open text field attached to a named business, on a page carrying Stayo's verification badge, is not something to accept from an anonymous visitor. The write box shows a sign-in prompt rather than a form that discards the writing at the end.
  2. **Nothing is public until an admin publishes it.** Every review lands `PENDING`; `/admin/reviews` is the only path to `PUBLISHED`. Editing a published review returns it to `PENDING` — otherwise "approve once, rewrite after" is an open door onto the page.
  3. **Moderation is Stayo's, not the owner's.** An owner choosing which reviews of their own hostel appear is a testimonial page, not a review system, and a reader can tell the difference.
  4. **One review per account per hostel**, enforced by a unique index rather than a hopeful check. A second opinion from the same account replaces the first.
  5. **No average below three reviews.** Two reviews averaging 3.0 say nothing about a hostel and everything about two people, while carrying the authority of a number printed beside a star. Below the threshold the reviews are shown and the score is not — the same honesty rule that produced `ratings_available: false`.
  6. **~~`stayed_here` is a badge, never a gate~~ — reversed 2026-08-19, same day.** The badge labelled the trustworthy reviews instead of excluding the rest, which left a hostel's public page open to anyone with an account and an opinion. **Only current (`ACTIVE`) and former (`FORMER_TENANT`) residents of that hostel may review it** — `INVITED`, `CANCELLED` and `EXPIRED` are invitations that were never taken up, and those people have never spent a night in the building. Enforced on the server (`review-eligibility.ts`), and reported in the GET payload so the box explains itself *before* anyone writes rather than refusing a finished review. `stayed_here` survives as the badge distinguishing a finished stay from one in progress.
  7. **Ratings are per-category (migration 072), and the overall star is derived from them.** Cleanliness, Food & mess, Safety, Staff & support, Value for money, Location — Airbnb's six, translated for a place people live in for a year. Food is only asked of a hostel that serves meals. The overall number is the mean of the answered categories rather than a separate question: asking someone to rate the same stay twice invites two different answers and makes the card's number arbitrary. The admin queue shows the breakdown, since a 1-star for food hidden under a 4 for location is a different moderation decision from a general grumble.
  8. **Reviewer identity is first name plus last initial**, and "A resident" when there is no name — never an email or a phone number, which is where "use whatever identifier we have" ends up.
  9. **The author is told where their review stands** — pending, published, or rejected with the reason. A review that silently vanishes reads as a lost submission and gets rewritten.
- **Consequences:** A new `hostel_reviews` table (migration 071) and a moderation queue someone has to actually work — an unread queue is a slower way of rejecting everything. Reviews are gathered per hostel and shown only on that hostel's listing; there is no cross-hostel reputation, no owner reply, and no rating on search cards yet.
- **Related:** [[Decisions#ADR-073|ADR-073]], [[Decisions#ADR-084|ADR-084]], [[Database]], [[APIs]], [[Features]], [[Changelog]].

## ADR-087: Owner Leads tab — Accept / Hold / Reject replaces the no-op "Follow up" button

- **Date:** 2026-08-20
- **Status:** accepted
- **Context:** The Leads tab's per-lead card had a "Follow up" button that did nothing but open the read-only `LeadDetailSheet` — no actual follow-up action existed anywhere in the code. The product ask was three decisive actions (Accept, Hold, Reject) so a lead always moves toward a real outcome. Investigation before writing any code found: `visitor_leads.status` is a plain string (no DB enum), with `LEAD_STATUSES` = NEW/INTERESTED/ROOM_VISITED/DECISION_PENDING/READY_TO_JOIN/INVITED/JOINED/LOST and no REJECTED or ON_HOLD value; a proper multi-note history table (`lead_notes`) already existed and was the obvious home for a hold reason; `admissionsService.convertToInvitation()` (the lead→tenant bridge) already existed, already guarded against duplicate conversion via `converted_tenant_id`, but had **zero live callers** anywhere in the frontend; and a fully-built `TENANT_ENQUIRY_REJECTED` WhatsApp template contract (builder, tests, fallback reason text) already existed in `enquiry-template-contracts.ts` — written for Discover-sourced rejections but never wired to any caller.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Three new status strings — `ACCEPTED`, `ON_HOLD`, `REJECTED` — added to `LEAD_STATUSES`, not a parallel status model.** Since the column is already a free-text string, this needed no migration. The pre-existing funnel statuses (INTERESTED/ROOM_VISITED/etc., driven by lead-activity scoring) are untouched; the new three are only reachable through the owner's explicit Accept/Hold/Reject action, never through the activity-based `nextStatusForActivity()` state machine.
  2. **A pure transition guard, `canTransitionLeadStatus()`** (`src/services/admissions/lead-transition-guards.ts`, mirroring the existing `platform-leads/lead-transition-guards.ts` shape): any open/in-funnel status or `ON_HOLD` → `ACCEPTED`/`ON_HOLD`/`REJECTED`; `REJECTED` is terminal (no reopening mechanism exists in the product, so none was added here either); a lead already `INVITED`/`JOINED` (already converted to a tenant) cannot be re-accepted/held/rejected.
  3. **Hold's reason message reuses `lead_notes`**, the table that already existed for exactly this — no new notes/messaging table. `updateStatus({status: "ON_HOLD", note})` requires a non-empty `note` and writes it in the same call.
  4. **No new API routes.** All three actions PATCH the existing `/api/leads/[id]` → `admissionsService.updateStatus()`, which already accepted an arbitrary `status`; it gained the guard above, the hold-note requirement, and a best-effort tenant notification on Reject (**not** Hold — see the same-day amendment below).
  5. **Reject reuses the existing, previously-dead `TENANT_ENQUIRY_REJECTED` WhatsApp template contract** rather than building a new notification path, routed through the generic `whatsAppTemplateDeliveryService.send()`, never fatal to the status write if WhatsApp fails or the template isn't yet approved in Meta (same non-blocking pattern as `platformLeadNotificationService`).
  6. **Accept is a two-step flow, not an inline tenant creation.** Clicking Accept only sets `status: "ACCEPTED"` and navigates the owner to the existing `InviteTenantWizard` (now accepting an optional `initialData`/`leadId`, pre-filled from the lead's name/phone/email/hostel — it previously had no pre-fill mechanism at all). Submitting the wizard is what actually creates the tenant, via the now-wired-up `convertToInvitation`, which is what performs the lead→tenant link (`converted_tenant_id`) and flips status to `INVITED`. This two-step shape mirrors what the product asked for (`Lead → Accept → Add Tenant → prefilled → owner confirms/creates`) and reuses `converted_tenant_id`'s existing guard as the duplicate-tenant safety net — accepting twice, or refreshing mid-flow (`?fromLead=<id>` survives a refresh, unlike router state), cannot create a second tenant.
  7. **Tenant activation was deliberately left alone on the security-sensitive point.** The activation flow's phone-OTP requirement stays exactly as-is: there is no existing trust chain that marks an enquiry-captured phone as pre-verified, so skipping OTP for a lead-accepted tenant would be a real auth weakening, which was explicitly out of bounds for this change. Name was already never editable during activation and hostel/room was already read-only — both requirements were already met with no code change. The one safe, non-weakening improvement made: the activation form's email field (previously always started blank) now pre-fills from the invitation's email when present, saving a retype without touching the Gmail-format validation or the OTP gate.
- **Amendment (same day, 2026-08-20): Hold no longer sends any tenant notification.** Originally shipped with a `TENANT_ENQUIRY_ON_HOLD` WhatsApp template mirroring Reject's, on the reasoning that both were tenant-facing status changes worth a message. Explicit product direction reversed this: Hold is an owner-internal note, not a tenant-facing action — it must only write the `lead_notes` row and change `status` to `ON_HOLD`. The now-unused `TENANT_ENQUIRY_ON_HOLD` template, its builder, and the `notifyEnquiryOnHold()` call site were removed rather than left dead, along with their tests. Reject's notification is unchanged. See point 5's revision above and [[Business-Rules#Admissions / Leads — Accept / Hold / Reject (2026-08-20)|Business-Rules]].
- **Consequences:** the Leads tab card lost its "Follow up"/"Chat" buttons (Chat was already a "Coming soon" stub) in favor of a tappable card + working WhatsApp/Call icons; `LeadDetailSheet` gained the Accept/Hold/Reject footer. `useAlerts.ts`'s lead fetch and `LeadDetailSheet`'s detail fetch were migrated from ad hoc `useState`/hand-rolled query keys onto the pre-existing-but-unused `queryKeys.admissions.*`, so the new mutations can invalidate by key instead of needing manual refetch plumbing — closing a real gap the codebase already had a convention for. `toEnquiryStage()` in `discovery-service.ts` (which projects `visitor_leads.status` into the seeker-facing Discover enquiry stage) gained a one-line fix mapping the new `REJECTED` status to `CLOSED`, alongside the pre-existing `LOST` case — without it, a Discover-sourced enquiry the owner rejects would have shown the seeker "still reviewing" forever. **Verified (post-amendment):** `npm run test:pure` (backend, 32 new tests across `admissions-lead-transition-guards.test.ts`, `admissions-lead-actions.test.ts`, and the extended `enquiry-template-contracts.test.ts`) and the full frontend suite (826 tests) pass; `check:architecture` and the frontend production build succeed; `check:invariants` shows one pre-existing, unrelated failure (`app/api/tenants/me/complete-profile` and `.../onboarding-settings` selecting a first-hostel fallback) confirmed present with this change's files fully reverted. **Not verified:** end-to-end against a live database or browser — no `DATABASE_URL_TEST` and no local TypeScript compiler in this environment (pre-existing gaps, unrelated to this change); `stayo_tenant_enquiry_rejected` (Reject's template, the only one remaining) is not yet approved in Meta, so that send will no-op (logged, non-fatal) until that's done outside this codebase.
- **Related:** [[APIs]] (Admissions/Leads section), [[Database]] (`visitor_leads.status`), [[Business-Rules]] (new Admissions/Leads section), [[Features]], [[Changelog]]. Renumbered from the originally-drafted ADR-085 to ADR-087 during the 2026-08-20 sync with upstream, which had independently used ADR-084/085/086 for its own share-links/mess-menu/reviews decisions — no content changed, only the number and its cross-references.

## See also
- [[Changelog]] for the chronological record of what shipped
- [[Architecture]] for the system these decisions govern
- [[Bugs]] for issues that prompted some of these decisions

## ADR-088: A hostel's navigation data is admin-owned platform data, not owner marketing content

**Date:** 2026-08-22 · **Status:** accepted

**Context.** Stayo's listings cluster around campuses, and in a dense cluster a hostel's
`address` resolves to the lane, not the building — five hostels share it. Phase 1 fixes this
with a **Google Place ID collected by hand** from Google's Place ID Finder, plus a landmark, a
photo of the entrance and a distance from a reference campus. No Google Maps API is used: no
key, no billing, no quota. The obvious home for this was `hostel_marketing_revisions.content`,
alongside the existing `places[]` "Getting around" list.

**Decision.**

1. **Navigation lives on `hostels.navigation` (jsonb), written only by `/api/platform-admin/*`.**
   Not in marketing content. That payload is authored by the owner and moves with the
   draft/review lifecycle — putting a Place ID there would let an owner point Stayo's
   directions at whatever building they liked, and would *withdraw* the Place ID along with a
   draft. `hostels` already encodes this line: `listing_status` carries a schema comment saying
   it is deliberately not owner-writable "so an owner writing it directly would self-approve
   past platform verification" (ADR-040). Navigation belongs on the same side of it.
2. **The Google Maps URL is never stored.** It is derived from `placeId` at render time. A
   stored URL is a second source of truth that goes stale silently, and the failure mode is a
   student standing on the wrong street.
3. **The distance is admin-entered too**, in the same object, rather than read from the owner's
   existing `places[]` SNIST row. The owner's row stays and keeps rendering; the navigation
   block's number is admin-owned so the whole block is trustworthy end to end. It is free text
   ("400m", "5 min walk") because a `numeric` metres column invites a precision nobody measured.
4. **`referenceName` is stored, not hardcoded.** "400m from SNIST" reads from data, so the first
   hostel Stayo lists near a different college is not a code change.
5. **Approval warns, it does not block.** Two hostels were already LIVE with no Place ID the day
   this shipped; a hard server-side gate would have made them unfixable through the normal flow
   and would have failed an admin mid-approval with no way forward. The admin drawer shows a
   loud "No Place ID — students get no directions" warning instead. Revisit once every live
   hostel has one.

**Consequences.** Owners cannot see or set navigation, and the owner marketing editor is not
told the field exists. Admins gain a Navigation block in the Listings drawer, deliberately
*outside* the review branch — navigation is hostel-level, not revision-level, and every hostel
on Stayo the day this shipped was already live with nothing pending.

A hostel nobody has located renders **no directions block at all**. That is the intended
failure: no button beats a button that opens the wrong building. `parseNavigation()` therefore
never throws, and a malformed row degrades to `null` rather than 500ing a public listing.

Related: [[Database#`hostels.navigation`]] · [[APIs]] · [[Features]] · ADR-040 · ADR-076

## ADR-089: A live listing has two pull-back levers, not one, and neither removes the hostel

**Date:** 2026-08-22 · **Status:** accepted

**Context.** `marketingReviewService.approve()` and `.reject()` both hard-require
`PENDING_REVIEW`. The moment a revision became `APPROVED` it left the console's reach
entirely: there was no transition out of `APPROVED` except being `SUPERSEDED` by a later
approval. An admin who spotted a wrong price on a live listing had exactly one lever —
`suspend-listing` — which removes a real, verified hostel from Discovery in order to fix a
sentence. Worse, that lever existed end to end on the backend *and* in the frontend API
wrapper and was **wired to no button anywhere in the app**, so in practice there was nothing.

**Decision.** Two post-approval actions, deliberately different in blast radius, because the
two real situations are different:

1. **Request changes** — the approved revision stays `APPROVED`, so the live page does not go
   dark while the owner gets round to it. A `DRAFT` seeded from the live content, carrying the
   reviewer's note and section flags, is waiting in their editor. For a typo, a stale menu, a
   weak photo: the listing being imperfect for another day beats it being blank.
2. **Unpublish** — the approved revision becomes `WITHDRAWN` and the listing immediately
   renders as a hostel that has not published details. For a false claim or a wrong price,
   where "still up while they fix it" is the wrong answer.

**Neither removes the hostel from Discovery.** Suspending remains the third and bluntest lever,
now actually reachable from the drawer.

**`WITHDRAWN` is a new revision status, not a reuse of `REJECTED`.** `REJECTED` means "an admin
read this and it never went live". `WITHDRAWN` means "this was live and we took it down" — a
different fact about what tenants actually saw. That distinction is the same reason `SUPERSEDED`
exists rather than deleting old revisions: anything that settles "but the listing said ₹4,500"
has to know which of the two happened.

**Request changes refuses while a submission is queued.** That submission is the thing to
review; quietly writing notes onto a different draft would leave the queue item unanswered and
the owner holding two sets of feedback about one listing. Unpublish is *allowed* in that state —
the live page and the queued submission are different things, and a false claim comes down now
regardless of what is waiting behind it.

**Consequences.** `getEditorState`'s `last_rejection` now also fires on `WITHDRAWN`, and carries
the status, because a blank listing with the reason visible nowhere in the product is the exact
state this ADR exists to avoid. Revision-status readers are confined to the two marketing
services, which is what made adding a status cheap.

Related: [[Business-Rules]] · [[APIs]] · [[Features]] · ADR-076 · ADR-040

### ADR-090 — The owner sees the payout pipeline, and every rupee in it has a state (2026-08-23)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Migration 070 built settlement for the **admin who executes it**. The owner — whose money it is — could see none of it; the only owner-facing route was `/api/owner/payout-account`. Worse, **nothing wrote `gateway_transactions`**: the Razorpay webhook never created a row, so the settlement engine had a reader and no writer (confirmed against live data — 0 gateway transactions, 0 successful payment attempts). The 2026-08-17 design listed owner-facing views as an explicit non-goal.
- **Decision:** Add an owner-facing "Money in" surface **inside the Money tab's Collections section**, not as a fourth tab, and wire gateway ingestion so it has a source. Every rupee is presented in exactly one of four states — **Owed / Paid to you directly / With Stayo / In your bank** — and the totals are *derived from* their parts rather than computed separately and validated, so the block reconciles by construction.
- **Consequences:**
  - **A `FAILED` transfer stays counted inside "With Stayo."** It is money Stayo still holds; a total that shrinks when a transfer fails is the fastest way to lose an owner.
  - **Rent paid directly to the owner is never summed into anything Stayo owes** — a display distinction only, settleability still comes solely from `gateway_transactions`.
  - Payout figures **ignore the hostel filter**: a payout is one bank transfer covering every hostel, so a filtered figure would match no line in the owner's passbook. Per-hostel attribution moved inside a payout's breakdown.
  - The word **"settlement" never appears in owner-facing copy** — same reasoning that renamed Obligations to Charges. A test asserts it across all five strip states.
  - The strip's voice priority is **failed › paid today › pending › settled › never**, enforced in a tested pure function rather than by JSX ordering.
- **See:** [[Business-Rules]], [[APIs]], [[Database]], [[Features]], `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-23-owner-money-in-design.md`

### ADR-091 — A payout carries a stored promise, not a recomputed estimate (2026-08-23)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** An owner's month is shaped by what goes *out* — building lease on the 1st–5th, staff on the 1st, mess supplier weekly — while rent arrives across the 1st–10th. He lives in that gap. A payout **date** is therefore the product, not a status detail, and *predictability beats speed*: he can plan around a consistent T+2 and cannot plan around "usually fast."
- **Decision:** `settlement_items.expected_payout_date` = capture date **+ 2 working days**, computed once at run creation and **stored**. Two working days rather than one because every transfer is still made by a human from a real bank, and a promise that needs everything to go right is not a promise.
- **Consequences:**
  - A promise derived at read time can never be *missed* — it silently moves. Stored, it becomes a fact `paid_at` is measured against, which is what lets "Last 8 payouts — all on time" be **false when it is false**.
  - The counter stays silent below two judged payouts, and reports "6 of the last 8" when a promise was missed. A counter that only ever reports good news is not evidence of anything.
  - Weekends are skipped. **Public holidays are deliberately not modelled** — there is no calendar in this system and Indian bank holidays are state-specific, so a payout falling on Diwali is reported late, honestly, rather than the promise being bent to cover it.
  - Items predating the column carry `NULL`, which the counter treats as *no promise was made* rather than a broken one.
- **See:** [[Business-Rules]], [[Database]], [[Decisions#ADR-090|ADR-090]]

### ADR-092 — Two columns that must not exist in the Prisma schema (2026-08-23)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** On 2026-08-22, declaring `navigation Json?` on `hostels` and deploying ahead of migration 074 500'd every hostel listing page in production — Prisma requests *all* declared scalars on any read without an explicit `select`, so one new field broke services that never asked for it. Migration 075 needed to add two columns to tables read on the payment path.
- **Decision:** `settlement_items.expected_payout_date` and `gateway_transactions.tenant_id` are added by migration 075 and **deliberately left out of `prisma/schema.prisma`**. All access to both is parameterised raw SQL.
- **Consequences:**
  - Application code is correct whether or not 075 has been applied, so deploy order stops being a production risk.
  - The gateway ingestion INSERT runs inside a **Postgres savepoint** — a failed statement aborts the entire transaction, so a plain try/catch would be a lie and the payment settlement would roll back with it. Concretely: without the savepoint, deploying ahead of 075 would have failed *every gateway payment*.
  - `items()` falls back to a promise-less query; the summary uses `Promise.allSettled` so one unreadable column cannot blank the other three sources.
  - This makes `src/services` a place that writes raw SQL, so the `no $queryRawUnsafe` invariant was **extended to scan `src/services`** — CLAUDE.md points new domain services there, meaning the newest financial code in the repo had been sitting entirely outside that rule.
- **See:** [[Database]], [[Architecture]], [[Bugs]]

### ADR-093 — An export asks who it is for, not what format you want (2026-08-23)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The existing `ExportExpensesModal` asked for a *scope* (current view / this month / all time) with the format hardcoded to Excel — a data-dump model. But looking at what an owner actually does with an export, **every single use is handing the file to another person**: an accountant, a bank officer, a business partner, a hostel manager. Only bank reconciliation is for himself. He has no opinion about CSV versus XLSX; he knows it is for his CA.
- **Decision:** The export sheet asks **what do you need it for** and offers four named documents — *For my accountant* (Excel), *Proof of income* (PDF), *Bank reconciliation* (Excel), *Who owes me* (PDF). Format, columns and period defaults are consequences of that answer, decided in code.
- **Consequences:**
  - **The settlements-versus-collections split dissolves.** The owner never picks a data domain; he picks what he is doing and the right rows follow. Four purposes span both, plus expenses.
  - **Scope widened to include expenses**, because "for my accountant" is income *and* expenses — a CA cannot file from income alone. `ExportExpensesModal` is **deleted** rather than left as a second, inconsistent way to export from the same tab.
  - The one capability dropped is the old modal's "Current view" scope — exporting exactly the filtered expense rows on screen. Purpose-first has no equivalent, and it was not re-added.
  - The export entry point moved to the **Money tab header**, available from all three tabs, because the document rather than the tab is what is being chosen.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Decisions#ADR-094|ADR-094]]

### ADR-094 — A document handed to a lender never blends verified and self-reported money (2026-08-23)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** *Proof of income* exists so an owner can evidence his earnings to a bank — hostel income is famously undocumented, and this is genuinely the highest-value export in the set. But most of an owner's rent is cash or direct UPI **that he typed in himself**. Only gateway-collected money carries a payment-provider reference a third party can confirm.
- **Decision:** The statement renders **two separate sections with their own subtotals** — *Verified by Stayo* ("collected through Stayo's payment gateway; each payment carries a payment-provider reference") and *Recorded by the owner* ("cash and direct UPI, entered by the owner. Stayo did not handle this money and cannot independently confirm it"). Never one blended figure with a footnote.
- **Consequences:**
  - A credit officer skims footnotes, so the **structure** has to carry the distinction rather than a note. Blending would present self-reported cash as third-party-verified income — the one way this feature could actively mislead someone.
  - **Both sections always render, even when empty.** An absent section would quietly turn a mixed statement into one that reads as entirely verified.
  - The rule is a pure function, `proofOfIncomeSections()`, not a string inside a PDF drawing call — a rule that only exists inside a renderer cannot be tested, and this one has to be.
  - Excluding owner-recorded money entirely was rejected: it would understate real income badly and help nobody.
- **See:** [[Business-Rules]], [[Features]], [[Decisions#ADR-093|ADR-093]]

### ADR-095 — Financial-year presets resolve on the server (2026-08-23)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** India's financial year runs **1 April to 31 March**. A "this year" export running January to December is not slightly wrong for an accountant — it is useless, and the owner does not find out until his CA calls him in July. It is the single most likely way this feature fails silently.
- **Decision:** Period presets are sent by **name** (`this_fy`, `last_fy`, `this_month`, `last_month`), never as resolved dates, and the server turns them into ranges via a pure, tested `financial-year.ts`. A custom range is **refused** if reversed or malformed rather than silently repaired.
- **Consequences:**
  - "This financial year" means April–March because the server says so, not because a browser agreed.
  - The January case gets its own test: in January the *current* FY began in the previous calendar year, which a naive `year - 1` gets wrong.
  - Silently repairing a reversed custom range would produce a document that looks right and covers the wrong period — worse than an error the owner can see and fix.
- **See:** [[Business-Rules]], [[APIs]]

### ADR-096 — Signing up takes an email and a password again, and the phone stays at the enquiry (2026-08-24)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** [[Decisions#ADR-078|ADR-078]] made tenant signup **Google-only** — `LoginModal`'s Sign Up tab was reduced to a single "Continue with Google" button. That removed a form, but it also made a Google account a hard prerequisite for using Stayo Discover at all: someone without one, or unwilling to hand one over to browse hostels, had **no way to create an account**. `AuthContext.signUpTenant()` and `POST /api/auth/tenant-signup` were left in place but unwired, so the capability existed and was simply unreachable.
- **Decision:** The Sign Up tab takes **name, email, password and confirm password**, submitting to the existing `/api/auth/tenant-signup`. "Continue with Google" stays, below an OR divider — two ways in, neither privileged.
- **What deliberately did *not* come back is the phone/OTP step.** ADR-078 moved phone verification from signup-time to **enquiry-time**, where the number is actually needed and the person has a reason to prove it. That stays. So `phone` becomes **optional** on `TenantSignupSchema`, on `authService.selfSignUpTenant()` and on `AuthContext.signUpTenant()`, and a password-created account is born the **same shape a Google-provisioned one already is** — `phone: null`, `phone_verified: false` — and gets its verified number from `EnquiryPage`'s inline confirm → OTP → send flow (`enquiryPhoneVerification.ts`).
- **Consequences / guardrails:**
  - When a caller *does* send a phone, `/api/auth/tenant-signup` still requires a fresh verified-or-skipped OTP for it (`resolveSignupPhoneVerification`). The loosening cannot be used to attach an **unverified** number to an account; it only allows **no** number.
  - `selfSignUpTenant` runs the duplicate-phone lookup **only when a phone was supplied**. `profiles.phone` is nullable *and* unique, so querying it with `null` would match the first phone-less account and reject every signup after it as a duplicate — the bug this guard exists to prevent.
  - Field rules live in a pure `@shared/lib/tenantSignupForm` module (`apps/frontend` has no jsdom, so the modal must stay a renderer). They **mirror** the backend's zod schema rather than replacing it — the server is still the authority; the client-side copy exists so someone isn't told what they got wrong only after a round trip.
  - The Google path is untouched, including the "plain login never auto-provisions" invariant of [[Decisions#ADR-031|ADR-031]].
- **Known gap, not closed here:** `POST /api/discover/enquiries` does **not** re-check `phone_verified` server-side — `EnquiryPage` is the only gate, and `discoveryService.createEnquiry` writes `student_phone: seeker.phone` whatever that is. A direct API call can therefore create an owner-visible lead with a null phone. This predates this change (it has been true since ADR-078 made `phone: null` the normal account shape) and is recorded in [[Bugs]].
- **See:** [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Business-Rules]], [[Decisions#ADR-078|ADR-078]], [[Decisions#ADR-035|ADR-035]]

### ADR-097 — Saving a floor's rooms is idempotent; the body is the floor, not a list of additions (2026-08-24)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** `POST /api/floors/:id/rooms` only ever *created*. The Add Hostel builder's Review screen offers an edit pencil on every floor, and taking it led back to a "Save floor & finish" button that re-posted rooms which already existed — answered with `CONFLICT: Room 101 already exists in this hostel`. Pressing Back into an already-saved floor did the same. **Every backward move past a saved floor was a dead end**, escapable only by abandoning the wizard.
- **Decision:** The request body is now the floor *as it should be*. Rooms that don't exist are created, rooms that do are updated, rooms the owner dropped are retired, and a retired room whose number comes back is revived rather than inserted twice.
- **Why revive rather than insert:** `@@unique([hostel_id, room_no])` covers inactive rows, so a retired room still owns its number. The old cross-hostel clash check filtered on `is_active: true` and would therefore have passed a number an inactive room held — then failed on the index with a raw Postgres error. That latent bug is closed by the same change.
- **Retired, never deleted.** `is_active: false`, because an old allocation, invitation or activity log may still reference the room.
- **Two refusals, both about people rather than data:** removing a room a tenant is allocated to, and shrinking a room below the number of tenants in it (which would leave someone allocated to a bed that no longer exists). Both name the room.
- **The decision is a pure function, `planFloorRoomSave`** (`lib/services/property/floor-room-plan.ts`), separate from the writes. This repo has no provisioned test database, so rules that live only inside a Prisma transaction cannot be tested at all — 20 tests cover the planner with no database. Same shape as `buildSettlementPlan` and the frontend's `floorBlocker`.
- **A live room on another floor is still a genuine conflict** and still 409s: two rooms claiming one number is something only the owner can resolve.
- **See:** [[APIs]], [[Business-Rules]], [[Bugs]], [[Features]]

### ADR-098 — A control boundary is not a divider: `--field-border` (2026-08-24)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Reported as "the fields to enter are not appearing" on Add Hostel. They were rendering. `stepStyles.textInput` drew each input as a transparent box with one `--border` underline, and `--border` (`#efe6da`) is **1.12:1** against the page it sits on — **fainter than the decorative graph-paper grid behind it** (`#ebdccf`, 1.21:1). WCAG asks 3:1 of a control boundary. The placeholder compounded it: set at the same weight as real input, "Sunrise Residency" read as an answer already given rather than an example.
- **Decision:** A new token, `--field-border: #9f8768`, for the visible edge of a form control — ≥3:1 against the page, the field fill and white, on both the product and marketing themes. `--border` stays as it is and stays correct for dividers and card outlines, where being quiet is the whole point. The two roles had been sharing one value and only one of them was being served.
- **Fields are now filled boxes** (`--input-background`, which existed and was unused here) with a focus ring, and placeholders drop to `font-normal` so an example never reads as an answer.
- **Consequence:** `muted-foreground` (3.54:1) and `warning` (3.28:1) are still under the 4.5:1 AA floor for body text across the product theme. Not changed here — that repaints every owner and tenant screen, and is its own piece of work. Recorded so it is not mistaken for settled.
- **See:** [[Frontend]], [[Features]], [[Bugs]]

### ADR-099 — A floor and a room can be deleted, and the copy says they are not coming back (2026-08-24)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** An owner could add a floor or a room and never remove it. `DELETE /api/floors/:id` and `DELETE /api/rooms/:id` both existed, fully guarded, with **no frontend caller anywhere**.
- **Correction to an earlier draft of this ADR:** it also claimed `DELETE /api/hostels/:id` had no caller. **That was wrong.** Archiving a hostel has been wired to the owner dashboard for a long time — hostel card menu → `HostelOptionsSheet` → `ArchiveHostelModal` → `useArchiveHostel()` (`features/settings/settingsHooks.ts`). The claim came from grepping for `deleteHostel` and never for `archiveHostel`. No hostel gap existed, and a briefly-built second removal card on the hostel Overview screen was deleted as a duplicate; that screen now opens the **existing** `ArchiveHostelModal` instead, so one destructive action keeps one confirmation UI.
- **Decision:** Delete affordances for floors and rooms, in the Rooms tab.
  - **Room** — inside the room sheet's edit mode, behind two taps. `prisma.rooms.delete` is a **real** delete, unlike a hostel archive, and the confirmation says so in as many words.
  - **Floor** — on an expanded floor, and **only when it is empty**, mirroring `deleteFloor`. Emptying it first is deliberate: deleting a floor must never become a way to delete rooms the owner has not looked at.
- **Each refusal names the fix, not just the failure.** An occupied room says move them out; a *reserved* one says cancel the invite — a different problem with a different fix, so a different sentence. A floor with rooms says delete the rooms first. Eligibility is the pure `propertyRemoval.ts`; the server stays the authority and its own message is surfaced when it refuses anyway.
- **This exposed a second bug:** the Rooms tab rendered `null` for a floor with no rooms, so the only floor that *could* be deleted was the one that could not be seen. Empty floors now render outside of search.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Business-Rules]], [[Bugs]]

### ADR-100 — A hostel that never carried anything can be deleted for good (2026-08-24)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** `DELETE /api/hostels/:id` archives, and the dashboard's Archived tab shows archived hostels with **Reactivate as their only action**. That is correct for a property that carried real tenancies — its payments and agreements must outlive it — but it meant a hostel created by mistake (a test entry, a typo, a duplicate) was parked in the owner's account **permanently, with no way out**. Reported by an owner looking at two junk hostels named "ABC Hostel" and "test".
- **Second correction to my own earlier claims.** ADR-099's first draft said archiving had no frontend caller (wrong — it is wired to the dashboard hostel menu), and the notes around it also said "nothing shows an owner their archived hostels, so restoring is a support operation" — **also wrong**. `useOwnerDashboard` fetches with `includeArchived: true` and `PropertyList` renders an ARCHIVED tab with a working `ReactivateHostelModal`. Both claims came from grepping for names the code does not use. The vault's own `Features.md` already recorded the archive wiring; reading it first, as CLAUDE.md instructs, would have prevented all of this.
- **Decision:** A second, narrower endpoint — `DELETE /api/hostels/:id/permanent`. A separate path rather than a `?permanent=true` flag, so it cannot be reached by accident and the archive route's contract is unchanged for every existing caller.
- **Two conditions, both server-enforced by the pure `planHostelDeletion`:**
  1. The hostel must **already be ARCHIVED**. Deletion is deliberately a two-step, so nothing live is destroyable in one press.
  2. It must have **no operational history at all** — zero tenants, payments, rent obligations, room allocations, agreements, receipts, expenses and enquiries. The refusal names the first thing found and says the hostel *stays archived and its history stays intact*, so "cannot be deleted" never reads as "your data is at risk".
- **Only structure is removed:** rooms, then floors, then the hostel, in that order inside one transaction — `rooms.hostel_id` and `floors.hostel_id` carry no `onDelete: Cascade`. `hostels` has 40+ child relations, so any FK this did not think to count surfaces as "still has records attached — it stays archived", never as a mystery 500.
- **The only irreversible action in the owner app**, so it asks for the hostel's name to be typed and says there is no undo.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Business-Rules]], [[Decisions#ADR-099|ADR-099]]

### ADR-101 — A third route category: public, but identity-aware (2026-08-24)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** `middleware.ts` knew two kinds of API route: public (identity headers **stripped**, no session ever visible) and authenticated (401 without a valid token). `PUBLIC_ROUTES` is prefix-matched, so `/api/discover/hostels` makes `GET /api/discover/hostels/:slug/reviews` public — correct, since anyone may read reviews. But that GET also returns *the reader's own pending review* and *whether they may write one*, and both need to know who is asking. With identity stripped, `getSeeker()` always returned null and eligibility always came back `SIGNED_OUT` — **a signed-in resident was shown "Sign in to write a review" while holding a perfectly good session.** [[Decisions#ADR-086|ADR-086]]'s writer-eligibility rule was effectively unreachable through the UI.
- **A near-identical bug had already been fixed for the write** (`requiresSessionDespitePublicPrefix`, for `POST .../reviews`). The read needed the opposite thing — not "require a session", but "accept one if offered" — which no category expressed.
- **Decision:** `allowsOptionalIdentity(pathname, method)`, alongside the existing exception in the same pure module. For a matching path, middleware runs the **same** verification as any authenticated route — Supabase JWT, legacy fallback, revocation deny-list, idle timeout — and passes the identity headers through on success.
- **The important half: every 401 on those paths becomes "continue anonymously" instead.** A stale token in someone's browser must not turn a public listing into an error, and a revoked one must not be honoured — so the checks are not relaxed, only their *failure mode* is. This is deliberately not a lenient copy of the verification path; reusing the real one is what keeps the revocation guarantee intact on a public page.
- **Scoped to one path and one verb.** Tests assert it does not leak to `/api/discover/enquiries`, `/api/discover/saved`, the listing route itself, or to any write.
- **See:** [[Bugs]], [[APIs]], [[Decisions#ADR-086|ADR-086]], [[Decisions#ADR-031|ADR-031]]

### ADR-102 — An empty reviews section is not rendered at all (2026-08-24)

- **Status:** Accepted — reverses a copy decision made with [[Decisions#ADR-086|ADR-086]]
- **Context:** With no reviews, the listing showed a card reading *"No reviews yet — only people who have lived at X can review it, and Stayo checks each one before it appears…"*, and beneath it a second card, *"Lived at this hostel? Sign in to write a review"*, with a Sign in button. The original reasoning was recorded in the component: "A blank space reads as neglect; this reads as a rule, which is what it is."
- **Decision:** Render nothing. No heading, no empty state, no explanation. The section appears when there are reviews to read, **or** when the person looking is a resident who can add one.
- **Why the earlier reasoning does not hold:** a visitor deciding between hostels is not owed a paragraph about Stayo's moderation policy in the place where reviews would be. Every new listing carried it, so the explanation was the *normal* state of the section rather than an exception — and it pushed the price and the Enquire button further down on every page it appeared on. The rule is still enforced; it simply is not narrated to people it does not apply to.
- **The locked write box goes too.** Someone who has never lived here now sees the reviews and nothing else, instead of a card explaining why they may not write one.
- **The section stays collapsed while loading**, rather than flashing a heading that then disappears.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[Decisions#ADR-086|ADR-086]], [[Decisions#ADR-101|ADR-101]]

### ADR-103 — Alerts search looks in all four lists, not the open one (2026-08-24)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The owner Alerts tab holds four lists behind four chips — Leads, Messages, Renewals, Requests — and had no search at all. The obvious implementation is a filter on whichever chip is open.
- **Why that is the wrong tool:** an owner looking for one person does not know which list they are filed under. Type "Riya" while standing on Leads, get "no results", and conclude Riya is not in Stayo — when she is in Renewals, one chip away. A filter scoped to the open tab makes the *tab you happened to be on* determine whether search works.
- **Decision:** One box above the chips searching **all four lists at once**. The open chip shows its matches; the **chip counts follow the query**, so the tabs become the result summary; and any other category with matches is offered as an "Also found in — Renewals (1)" chip that switches to it.
- **Matching rules, all in the pure `alertsSearch.ts`:**
  - **Every word must match, anywhere in the record**, in any order and across field boundaries — the owner does not know which field holds what. Fields are joined with a separator so a token cannot match across the seam between two of them.
  - **A phone is matched numerically, never as text.** Both the stored number and the typed one are reduced to their last 10 digits (`toLocalPhone`), so `8046`, `8008046952` and `918008046952` all find `+918008046952`. Digits are matched **only** against phone fields and **only** from three digits up — with the number in the text haystack, typing `80` matched it as a plain substring, and `80` matches roughly every Indian mobile in the account. Both defects were caught by the tests, not by review.
  - **Labels are searched, not raw values.** A lead reads "Ready to join" on screen, so that is what gets typed — never `READY_TO_JOIN`.
  - Below two characters the query is inert and every list passes through whole, matching the server-side rule in [[Decisions#ADR-044|ADR-044]].
- **Deliberately local, not a new endpoint.** These four lists are already in memory and are short; a round trip per keystroke would be slower and would need a search index for data that fits on one screen. `/api/owner/search` (ADR-044) remains the right tool for the whole account — it covers tenants, hostels and rooms, and does **not** cover leads, which is why this is not simply routed through it.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[Decisions#ADR-044|ADR-044]]

### ADR-104 — Accepting an enquiry and inviting the tenant are one act (2026-08-24)

- **Status:** Accepted — supersedes part of [[Decisions#ADR-087|ADR-087]]
- **Context:** The owner's Accept button PATCHed the lead to `ACCEPTED` and *then* opened the pre-filled Add Tenant wizard. It had to: `convertToInvitation` hard-required `status === "ACCEPTED"`. **Close that wizard without sending, and the lead stayed marked Accepted with no invitation behind it.** Reported by an owner looking at four enquiries all reading "Accepted" that nobody had actually been invited to — and nothing in the product distinguishes that state from a real acceptance.
- **Decision:** Accept writes nothing. The button (now **"Accept & invite"**) opens the wizard, and the lead advances — straight to `INVITED` — inside `POST /leads/:id/convert-to-invitation`, when the invitation really goes out. Abandoning the wizard leaves the enquiry exactly as it was, still awaiting a decision.
- **The backend guard had to move for this to be possible.** `status === "ACCEPTED"` became `canConvertLeadToInvitation(status, converted_tenant_id)` — a pure guard asking "is this still open to conversion" rather than "has it been accepted". It allows every open status, `ON_HOLD`, and `ACCEPTED`; it refuses `REJECTED`, `LOST`, `INVITED`, `JOINED` and anything already carrying a `converted_tenant_id`, each in its own words.
- **`ACCEPTED` stays allowed** because leads parked there by the old flow must still be convertible — including the four the owner was looking at when they reported this. The sheet's "Continue to Add Tenant" is the path for them.
- **`ON_HOLD` is now allowed too**, which the old rule refused. Sending an invitation is a stronger commitment than accepting, so demanding an un-hold first is ceremony — and with the eager PATCH gone, an on-hold lead whose owner pressed Accept would otherwise dead-end at the wizard's submit.
- **Nothing is lost by not writing ACCEPTED:** it sends no notification (only `REJECTED` does — an accepted enquiry is followed by the invitation, which carries its own message), so the removed PATCH cost the tenant nothing.
- **A test asserting the old rule was deleted, not adapted around.** `admissions-lead-actions.test.ts` had "refuses to convert a lead that has not been accepted"; it now asserts the opposite, with a comment saying why.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[Bugs]], [[Decisions#ADR-087|ADR-087]]

### ADR-105 — The Leads list is grouped by what is still owed, not by arrival (2026-08-24)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Alerts → Leads was one flat list in arrival order with every card styled identically. An owner with four settled enquiries and one new one had to read all five to find the one that needed them, and a new enquiry landed *among* the settled ones rather than above them. The screenshot that prompted this showed four "Accepted" cards filling the screen, each offering **Call** and **WhatsApp** as though there were still a conversation to have.
- **Decision:** Five groups, ordered by urgency, each stating what it is for. **Needs you** (open statuses) and **Accepted — invitation not sent** open by default; **On hold**, **Invited & joined** and **Closed** collapse to a header line with a count. An empty group does not render at all.
- **`awaiting_invite` is its own group, not filed under done.** A lead marked ACCEPTED with no invitation sent is half-finished, and the old "Accepted" pill made it look complete — the exact confusion behind [[Decisions#ADR-104|ADR-104]]. The group is named after the **missing step**, and its hint says outright: "They are not tenants until the invitation goes out." A test asserts that label, because the temptation to shorten it back to "Accepted" is what created the problem.
- **The primary button is that lead's actual next step**, not the same button everywhere: *Accept & invite* while undecided, *Send invitation* once accepted, *Review* while on hold, and **no primary button at all** for a lead with nothing outstanding. Previously WhatsApp held the dark position on every card, so the most prominent thing on a finished enquiry was a conversation there was no reason to start. Call and Chat remain as the quieter pair.
- **"Needs you" is ordered by `lead_score`, everything else by recency.** The funnel already scores a lead (a room visit and a join request both move it), so the person most likely to take a bed sits at the top rather than whoever enquired last. Settled groups are not being ranked, they are being looked up. Ties fall back to recency and then to id, so the order cannot wobble between renders.
- **An unrecognised status surfaces in "Needs you" rather than disappearing** into a collapsed group — a lead must never become invisible because of a status this build has not heard of.
- **A search overrides collapse.** Typing a name, seeing the count say 1, and finding nothing on screen would make the search built alongside this ([[Decisions#ADR-103|ADR-103]]) look broken; the owner's own toggle still wins over both.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[Decisions#ADR-103|ADR-103]], [[Decisions#ADR-104|ADR-104]]

### ADR-106 — The Leads inbox is fetched in two halves, because score-ordering can hide a new enquiry (2026-08-24)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The Alerts tab fetched `admissionsService.list({ limit: 20 })` — one page, one list. Two things were wrong with that, and the second is the serious one.
  1. `limit` is clamped to **50** server-side, so leads beyond that were simply absent.
  2. `listLeads` orders by **`lead_score desc`, then `last_activity_at desc`** — *not* by whether anything is owed. A brand-new enquiry scores low, so it sorts **below** settled high-score leads. With enough finished work in the account, **a new enquiry falls off the end of the page and the owner never sees it.** Collapsing settled groups ([[Decisions#ADR-105|ADR-105]]) hides that from view without fixing it.
- **Decision:** Fetch the inbox as two pages, not one — actionable statuses (open + `ACCEPTED` + `ON_HOLD`) and settled ones (`INVITED`, `JOINED`, `REJECTED`, `LOST`) — via a new `statuses=` multi-value filter on `GET /api/leads`. The actionable half is then bounded by how much work is genuinely outstanding rather than by how much history sits above it.
- **`statuses` validates against `LEAD_STATUSES` and drops what it does not recognise**, and a filter naming nothing valid is **ignored rather than silently matching every lead** — the failure mode that would quietly undo the whole point.
- **The settled half is paged, not limit-grown.** A "Show older" that raised `limit` past 50 would be silently clamped back to 50 and the button would appear to do nothing; `useInfiniteQuery` with `page` is what actually advances.
- **The status sets are exported from `leadInbox.ts`**, the same module that does the grouping, so the fetch and the display cannot drift apart. A test asserts every actionable status lands in an open-by-default group, every settled one in a collapsed group, and that the two sets partition all eleven statuses exactly.
- **Truncation is stated, not hidden.** If the actionable half ever exceeds one page the list says so and points at search — this is the half where an unseen lead costs the owner money, so it must never fail silently.
- **Not fixed here:** the server-side ordering itself. Making `listLeads` sort actionable-first needs either a status-priority `CASE` in raw SQL or a stored column, and neither belongs in this change. Splitting the fetch achieves the same guarantee without touching the query plan.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Decisions#ADR-105|ADR-105]]

### ADR-107 — Home shows money out, and calls it "Left" rather than profit (2026-08-24)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The owner home page was entirely money **in** — "August Collection, ₹16,000 of ₹57,000". That answers *how much have I collected* and never the question an owner actually opens the app with: **am I ahead this month?** ₹16,000 collected means one thing against ₹4,200 of spending and quite another against ₹30,000 of it. Expenses existed as a full subsystem and appeared nowhere on the first screen.
- **Decision:** The Collection card becomes the **month** card — Collected / Spent / **Left** — with the bar showing what has already gone out eaten from what came in. Plus one Action Center line in a month where a category spiked, and nothing there otherwise.
- **"Left", never "profit".** The figure is cash received minus cash spent: it ignores unpaid dues, deposits held, and anything accrued. Naming it profit would be the same error this codebase already refuses elsewhere (the hostel builder shows no revenue figure at all rather than deriving one from `base_rent`). A month where spending exceeded collection is an ordinary month for a hostel — a deposit refunded, a roof repaired — so the card says **"Overspent by ₹X"** rather than rendering "Left −₹2,300", which reads as a bug.
- **The anomaly rule is stricter on Home than on Money.** `expense-service` already annotates every category with `anomaly: previous > 0 && trend >= 35`, which has **no absolute floor** — ₹100 → ₹200 is +100% and fires. Among nineteen rows on a details page that is tolerable noise; on Home it is the only thing said about money going out, so a false alarm costs trust in the whole surface. The new `detectSpendAnomaly` adds a ₹1,000 floor, requires **both** floors rather than either, ranks by **rupees risen rather than percentage** (a ₹22,000 rise on groceries beats a 300% rise on stationery), refuses to speak without a prior-month baseline, and returns **one** category. The Money screen's own rule is left alone — different surface, different tolerance, and changing it was not asked for.
- **Composed in the route, not added to `portfolioService`.** That service documents its own invariant — every metric from `hostel_daily_snapshots`, no raw transactional table queried inside it — and `expenses` is a raw transactional table. The automated check names only payments, obligations and tenants, so putting the query there **would have passed the script while breaking the rule the script exists to protect**. `expenseService.getMonthSpendSummary()` (4 queries, against `getAllExpenses`'s ~13) is composed into `/api/owner/portfolio/summary` alongside the existing call.
- **The spend half is allowed to fail alone.** It is `.catch(() => null)` in the route, and the card renders collection-only when it is absent — rather than showing "Spent ₹0", which would be a claim rather than an absence. Home's tenants, beds and collection numbers matter more than this card.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Business-Rules]]

### ADR-108 — Floors are switchable, so finishing sweeps up what the walk missed (2026-08-24)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The Add Hostel builder walked floors strictly forward — Back one, "Save floor & continue" one. A floor's position in the queue was its only identity, so correcting a room on the ground floor from the second meant reversing through everything in between.
- **Decision:** A floor strip at the top of the Rooms step. Tapping a chip switches floors; nothing is written on a switch, because every floor's rooms already live in local state.
- **Free navigation removes a guarantee the linear walk gave for free**, and that is the real substance of this change. You previously *could not* reach floor 3 without floor 1 having been written on the way past. Now you can — fill the ground floor, tap across to the second, press finish — so **finishing sweeps every floor still holding unsaved rooms**. Without that, the switcher would silently lose work, which is the worst thing this screen could do.
- **The sweep is only cheap because saving is idempotent** ([[Decisions#ADR-097|ADR-097]]). Re-saving a floor that is already written is a no-op rather than the `Room 101 already exists` conflict it used to be, so the sweep does not have to reason about which floors it has already touched.
- **Validated in full before it writes anything.** `sweepBlocker` checks every floor about to be saved and, on failure, switches to the offending floor and names it — writing two floors and then failing on a third would leave the owner looking at a screen that reports neither.
- **Sequential, not parallel.** Each floor is its own request; a failure part-way should leave the earlier floors written rather than racing several writes at once.
- **Each chip carries its own state** — ✓ written, ● entered but not yet written, plain = still needs rooms — so the strip is the progress map as well as the navigation. Free movement is only safe if the owner can see what is outstanding.
- **The primary button follows the same rule.** It used to go to `activeIndex + 1`; with a switcher that marches past floors already filled and stops short of ones that were skipped. `primaryFloorAction` continues to the next floor still *needing rooms*, wrapping to the start, and says "finish" only when none do.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[Decisions#ADR-097|ADR-097]]

### ADR-109 — The floor form is three questions, and the numbering picker actually renumbers (2026-08-24)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** An audit of the Rooms step after the floor strip landed. It turned up one functional bug and a layout that had accreted rather than been designed.
- **The bug: the numbering picker did nothing once rooms existed.** `onPatternChange` was wired straight to `setPattern`, and the pattern only ever reached `resizeFloorRooms`, which numbers rooms *as it creates them*. So an owner who set the room count first and picked a scheme second tapped a chip, watched it highlight, and saw the list below keep `101, 102, 103`. Worse, the pattern is **one value for the whole builder** while rooms are numbered per floor at generation time — changing it on the second floor left the first on the old scheme, one building numbered two ways, with no warning.
- **Fixed by `renumberBuilding`**, applied across every floor when the scheme changes. A room is renumbered **only if its current number is exactly what the old scheme would have produced for its position**; anything else was typed by the owner, and their name for a room outranks a scheme. It tests the number rather than the `customised` flag on purpose — that flag is set by editing rent or sharing too, and changing a room's price is not a claim on its name.
- **The layout: five things told the owner where they were.** Progress bar, journey label, floor strip, eyebrow, and the footer summary — stacked, so the first control sat **42% down a phone screen**. The eyebrow is gone, and the journey label no longer repeats the floor name (the strip highlights it and the heading says it). The strip added last change was itself part of the problem; this removes what it replaced.
- **Three cards, each answering one question** in the order an owner asks it: *how many*, *what are they like*, *what are they called*. Previously only the middle block was carded and the count, numbering and list were bare, so nothing on the screen indicated which controls belonged together.
- **Things moved to where they are about.** The tally sits beside the counter it describes instead of at the foot of the list, where it read as a fourth room row. Numbering moved into the rooms card, labelled "Numbered across the whole building" so its scope is stated rather than implied by position. "Make the next floor the same as this" — the biggest time-saver on the screen — moved out of an unsectioned float above the footer into the card holding the rooms it copies, and now names them: "Copy these 3 rooms to the next floor".
- **Teaching copy appears only while it teaches.** "Set the usual room for this floor, then change the ones that differ" now shows on an empty floor and disappears once there are rooms — by the third floor it was occupying screen rather than helping.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[Bugs]], [[Decisions#ADR-108|ADR-108]]

### ADR-110 — Onboarding collects what we don't have, and re-verifies nothing we already proved (2026-08-25)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** A tenant invited off a Stayo Discover enquiry was met by the Identity screen as a stranger. It asked them to OTP the mobile we had *just WhatsApp'd the invitation link to*, then asked for a "Gmail ID" and refused the one they gave — `An account with this email address already exists` — because it was their own. The cause was a single dropped link: `convertToInvitation` records `converted_tenant_id` on the lead but never carries the person's identity onto the tenancy, and `createInvitation` writes `profile_id: null`. Everything downstream then behaved correctly on wrong information — `saveAccount`'s `isAlreadyVerified` check was already right, and returned false only because `profile` was null. See [[Bugs]].
- **Decision — three rules:**
  1. **Identity resolves the invitee's existing account** rather than assuming a new one. In order: already bound → the enquiry's `seeker_profile_id` (via `visitor_leads.converted_tenant_id`, already written and already indexed) → a guarded contact match → nobody. No new column, and the link is *resolved* rather than written at invite time — see the guardrail below.
  2. **A number we can already vouch for is not re-verified.** Two independent proofs, either sufficient: the linked account verified that exact number (every Discover seeker, at enquiry time), or the invitation link was delivered to it over WhatsApp. **Editing the number drops both and re-arms the OTP** — a changed number is one nothing was ever sent to.
  3. **The email is no longer collected.** It is derived from the account, else the invitation, else a phone-derived `@hms.temp` placeholder. The `@gmail.com`-only restriction goes with the field; it rejected addresses people had legitimately signed up with.
- **Consequences / guardrails:**
  - **Binding still happens at activation, not at invite.** Setting `tenants.profile_id` when the invitation is created would be simpler, but binding is what makes a person "taken": `tenants_one_live_tenancy_per_profile` and `assertCanStartNewTenancy` key off it, and the product deliberately lets someone hold invitations from competing hostels until they activate one. Binding early would let the first owner to send an invite win by default.
  - **`saveAccount` now keys its delegation on `!tenant.profile_id`, not `!profile`.** Those were the same question before this change and are not any more. `startActivation` is the only path that binds, and binding is what runs the eligibility check; short-circuiting past it because an account was *found* would leave the tenancy unbound and the one-live-tenancy rule unchecked.
  - **Contact match needs two agreeing signals.** An account found by the invitation's email is adopted only if the invitation was addressed to *that account's verified phone*. An owner mistyping an address that belongs to a real user would otherwise hand over that person's login, documents and residency history. Email matching with a disagreeing phone raises a conflict the owner must fix — it neither adopts nor creates.
  - **Delivery is recorded, not inferred, and only on success.** New column `tenant_invitations.whatsapp_delivered_at` (migration `20260825090000`). Everything else — a failed send, the email fallback, a row predating the column — reads back as unknown and is treated as untrusted. The rare fallback path therefore needs no branch of its own and cannot rot.
  - **The column is deliberately NOT declared in `schema.prisma`.** ~52 reads of `tenant_invitations` pass no explicit `select`, so declaring it would make every one of them demand a column that does not exist until the migration is applied — the [[Decisions#ADR-102|2026-08-22]] outage exactly. It is read and written by raw SQL in `invitation-delivery-trust.ts`, degrading to "unknown" when absent, which is also the safe answer.
  - **Fail-closed everywhere.** An absent `phone_trust` in the activation context, an unreadable column, an older backend — all mean "ask for the OTP".
- **See:** [[Features]], [[Bugs]], [[Business-Rules]], [[APIs]], [[Database]], [[Changelog]], [[Decisions#ADR-034|ADR-034]], [[Decisions#ADR-070|ADR-070]], [[Decisions#ADR-096|ADR-096]]

### ADR-111 — Onboarding does not ask what the hostel already answers, and the sky theme owns text contrast (2026-08-25)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Three problems on the Identity screen, reported from screenshots. (1) It asked every invitee for their **gender**, when a boys'/girls' hostel has already answered that by admitting them. (2) The **date of birth** field was an `<input type="date">` carrying `[&::-webkit-calendar-picker-indicator]:opacity-0` — the picker affordance was hidden, so it rendered as a dead-looking `mm/dd/yyyy` with nothing to tap, in month-first order that is wrong for an Indian product. (3) The wizard body renders **directly over the time-of-day sky gradient** with no surface of its own, but the step components used fixed warm greys (`#7A6F63`, `#8A7F75`) chosen for the daytime cream — so field labels faded into the dusk and night gradients, at exactly the hours people open an invitation link.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Gender is derived when the hostel type states it, and asked otherwise.** `BOYS → Male`, `GIRLS → Female`, field not rendered. `CO_LIVING`, `WORKING_PROS` and an **unset** type all still ask. Resolved server-side (`identity-field-policy.ts`) and published on the activation context as `identity_fields`, so the form and the validation that accepts it cannot drift.
  2. **The date of birth is a three-column day/month/year sheet, not a calendar.** For a birth date the current month is never the answer, so a month-paging calendar opens roughly 240 taps from where anyone needs to be.
  3. **`skyEnv()` gains `onSkyTitle` / `onSkyBody` / `onSkyLabel` / `onSkyShadow`**, consumed through a new `SkyProvider` context. Text *over the gradient* follows the hour; text inside an opaque card keeps its dark ink.
- **Consequences / guardrails:**
  - **A recorded gender always beats a derived one.** Someone recorded as "Other" in a boys' hostel is not silently rewritten to "Male" on their next save.
  - **Never guess from an unset type.** `hostels.hostel_type` is a nullable String and was **NULL on half the production hostels** when this shipped, so deriving a default would write a gender nobody supplied into a permanent tenant record. Today this means 1 of 4 live hostels hides the field; making the rest hide it is a **data cleanup, not a code change**.
  - **The frontend fails safe:** an absent `identity_fields` means ask (`genderRequired = true`).
  - **DOB parsing avoids `new Date()`** for `YYYY-MM-DD`, which applies the viewer's timezone and shifts the day west of UTC — the "birthday one day early" bug. Day/month/year are parsed as integers.
  - **The picker paints its own opaque surface** rather than inheriting the sky, because a modal decision should not get harder to read at night.
  - **`useSky()` falls back to the daytime palette** outside a provider, so a component rendered in isolation gets dark-on-cream rather than white-on-white.
  - **`validateDateOfBirth` returns one flat shape, not a discriminated union** — this project's tsconfig is not strict, so `ok: true | false` does not narrow at call sites and every consumer would need a cast.
- **Not done here:** capturing gender at *enquiry* time onto the profile (would need a `profiles.gender` column and an enquiry-side gate, and would let even co-living invitees skip the question). Deferred deliberately; the derive-or-ask rule needs no migration.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Business-Rules]], [[Frontend]], [[Changelog]], [[Decisions#ADR-110|ADR-110]], [[Decisions#ADR-070|ADR-070]]

### ADR-112 — The agreement's term is stated, committed to explicitly, and recorded — as a promise, never a threat (2026-08-25)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The tenant Agreement screen showed the hostel rules and two signature pads, and **never once stated how long the stay was for**. A tenant could sign an eleven-month commitment without the number "11" appearing anywhere on screen; the owner received a signature carrying no evidence the person had understood the length. The data existed the whole time — `Agreement.agreement_duration_months`, `agreement_start_date`, `agreement_end_date`, `contract_rent`, `contract_security_deposit`, populated on **8/8** rows in production — it simply was never included in the activation context payload.
- **Decision — the term becomes a ceremony in three beats:**
  1. **Before signing, "Your word" card.** The term as a headline (`11 months`, `1 Sep 2026 → 31 Jul 2027`) and **two columns**: what the tenant commits to, and what the hostel commits to (the room held for the full term, rent that does not move, the deposit returned at settlement).
  2. **On Confirm, the commitment sheet.** A first-person restatement — *"I am committing to stay at Sri Adithya Boys Hostel for 11 months — from 1 Sep 2026 until 31 Jul 2027."* — with **two checkboxes, neither pre-ticked**: read the agreement, and give the word. The primary action reads **"I give my word"**.
  3. **After signing, the handshake.** Both names and the term, instead of silently advancing.
- **Consequences / guardrails:**
  - **It never threatens, and this is load-bearing.** `AgreementTemplate.notice_period_days` is nullable and is **NULL on every live template**, so `move-out-service` records **no** notice violation — a tenant can raise a move-out at any time. Copy implying a penalty or a lock-in would be **false**, and a commitment screen caught bluffing destroys the trust it exists to build. The sheet states the exit path plainly: *"this is a promise, not a trap."* A test asserts no promise line matches `/penalt|forfeit|cannot leave|lock-?in/i`.
  - **Both halves are shown, always.** Listing only the tenant's obligations reads as extraction. Every line on the hostel's side derives from contract terms already stored on the agreement, so none of it is a marketing claim.
  - **The acknowledgement is recorded, or it is theatre.** Stored on `Agreement.content_snapshot` (Json, already "the resolved snapshot of everything") with the acknowledgement time, IP, user agent, and **the exact sentence the tenant read**. **No migration** — which matters because migrations here are applied by hand.
  - **The server validates against its own lifecycle, not the client's numbers.** `buildCommitmentRecord` takes the term from the freshly computed `lifecycle`, so the commitment is recorded against the real dates rather than a length the browser proposed.
  - **The statement is generated identically on both sides** (`agreement-commitment.ts` and `commitmentTerm.ts`) and a test pins them equal — what is stored must be what was shown.
  - **A hostel with no stateable duration is unaffected.** `hasStatableTerm` returns false for a null or zero duration and the ceremony is skipped entirely; signing behaves exactly as before rather than being blocked by a promise that cannot be worded.
  - **Dates are formatted from integer parts, never `new Date(iso)`** — the same timezone trap [[Decisions#ADR-111|ADR-111]]'s date-of-birth field avoids. A window shown one day off from the one recorded against someone's name would be worse here than anywhere else.
- **Not done here:** making the commitment mean anything *operationally* — setting `notice_period_days` and deciding what early exit costs. That touches move-out and settlement and is a deliberate, separate decision.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Business-Rules]], [[Frontend]], [[Changelog]], [[Decisions#ADR-111|ADR-111]], [[Decisions#ADR-059|ADR-059]]

### ADR-113 — A scheduled meal is a collection of dishes; the old single-item field becomes a denormalized cache, not a deletion (2026-08-24)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** `food_schedule_meals` modeled one day+meal-type cell as exactly one dish (`menu_item_id`/`item_name` scalars) — real hostel meals are combinations ("Rice, Dal, Curry, Chutney"), and picking a second item silently overwrote the first. Two constraints shaped the fix: the weekly grain (`@@unique([schedule_id, day_of_week, meal_type])` — one cell per day+meal) had to stay exactly as-is, and `src/services/marketing/mess-import.ts` reads `food_schedule_meals.item_name` **directly** (a raw `select`, no service in between) for a one-time copy into a hostel's Discovery-listing mess menu ([[Decisions#ADR-077|ADR-077]]) — out of scope for this change, and a real, live dependency on that exact column.
- **Decision:** A new child table, `food_schedule_meal_items` (ordered, `display_order`-keyed, FK to the parent cell with `onDelete: Cascade`), holds the real content — a cell is now one `food_schedule_meals` row with N ordered items, not N rows. `food_schedule_meals.menu_item_id`/`.item_name` are **not dropped**. They become an auto-maintained denormalized cache — first item's id, all item names comma-joined in display order, written on every mutation of the cell's items — so the marketing importer, and anything else still reading the legacy shape, keeps working with zero code changes.
- **Consequences:**
  - The PATCH contract (`PATCH .../meals/[mealId]`) becomes full-replace-the-item-list (`{menuItemIds: string[]}`) rather than single-overwrite — the same "PATCH replaces the whole cell" mental model, pluralized, with one caller in the repo so no back-compat shim was needed.
  - The swap endpoint's optimistic-concurrency guard, previously a `menu_item_id` equality check (a complete state check only because there was exactly one scalar), moves to `updated_at` equality — the only column that stays a full-state fingerprint regardless of item count, and one already bumped by every meal-content write.
  - `publishChecks.ts`'s "dominant item" and "repeats on consecutive days" warnings compare cells by an **order-independent set of dishes**, not the joined display string — a reorder-only edit must not make an otherwise-identical repeat invisible to those checks.
  - The migration is purely additive (new table + a backfill copying every existing cell's single item into one `display_order=0` child row) — no column ever gets dropped by this change, so there is nothing here that can lose data.
  - Two systems that already handle "several items" were deliberately left alone: `food_votes` (multi-select **voting** on what to serve next month — a different concept from what's actually being served) and `hostel_marketing_revisions.content.mess` (the Discovery listing's own reviewed copy). Neither was touched.
- **See:** [[Business-Rules]], [[Database]], [[APIs]], [[Food]], [[Decisions#ADR-077|ADR-077]]
