### ADR-114 — Manual-only food scheduling: auto-generator, cross-day swap-drag, and monthly carry-forward removed (2026-08-25)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Food scheduling had grown three separate pieces of automation on top of the owner's actual weekly menu: a vote-share/alphabetical **generator** (`assignWeekForMealType`, `POST /api/food/schedules/generate`) with a rebuild-mode policy (`decideRebuild`: `BUILD`/`FILL_GAPS`/`START_OVER`); a **cross-day swap-drag** (`POST .../meals/swap`, `canSwap`) letting the owner drag one day's meal onto another day's same-meal-type cell; and a nightly **carry-forward cron** that cloned last month's PUBLISHED schedule into a new DRAFT for any hostel missing a current-month row. Product direction changed: the owner should build the week by hand, dragging Food Library items into day/meal slots directly — no algorithm should ever choose, rank, or copy a dish on the owner's behalf. Usefully, the underlying schema already fit this model without change: `food_menu_items` was already the reusable library, and `food_schedule_meals`/`food_schedule_meal_items` ([[Decisions#ADR-113|ADR-113]]) already stored *ordered references* to library items per cell, not duplicated content — so this was a removal-and-reshape of the write paths, not a data-model rewrite.
- **Decision:**
  - Deleted outright: `lib/services/food-schedule-generator.ts`, `lib/services/food/schedule-rebuild-policy.ts`, `app/api/food/schedules/generate/route.ts`, `lib/services/food/meal-swap.ts`, `app/api/food/schedules/[id]/meals/swap/route.ts`, and their tests. The frontend's matching 7-day grid, tap-checklist picker sheet, and cross-day drag (`WeeklyScheduleGrid.tsx`, `DayRow.tsx`, `ScheduleMealPickerSheet.tsx`, `dragSwap.ts`) were replaced by a single Timetable page (`TimetablePage.tsx`) with day tabs, one active meal section at a time, and a search-and-drag (or tap) Food Library panel scoped to that section (`timetableDnd.ts`).
  - The carry-forward cron's schedule-cloning responsibility was removed; the cron itself survives, renamed `food-carry-forward` → `food-expiry`, keeping only its unrelated voting-period/poll auto-close jobs. A hostel's schedule row for a month is now created on demand — `POST /api/food/schedules`, a plain idempotent "create 28 empty cells if none exist yet" call with **no dish-selection logic and no copying from any other month**. Every month the owner navigates to starts genuinely empty; this is what makes it safe to call automatically on every month-navigation without risk of silently seeding content the owner didn't choose.
  - `PATCH .../meals/[mealId]` (full-replace-the-ordered-item-list) remains the **sole write primitive** for a cell — add, remove, and reorder from the new drag UI are all just different array computations feeding the same endpoint. It gained an `expectedUpdatedAt` optimistic-concurrency guard (conditional `updateMany` on `id`+`updated_at`, `409 STALE_WRITE` on mismatch) — the exact technique the now-deleted swap endpoint used, reused here now that the swap route is gone, so a stale edit from another tab can't silently clobber a newer one.
  - **Publish-gating philosophy reversal, scoped to completeness only:** `publishChecks.ts` previously returned only `PASS`/`WARN` for all three checks and its own header comment argued deliberately against ever blocking Publish ("a publish button that a check can disable is a product arguing with its owner"). That reasoning holds for `variety`/`runs` — whether Dosa every day is fine is the owner's call — but not for an outright empty cell, which isn't a stylistic choice, it's the literal "Not set" placeholder tenants would otherwise see live. `buildPublishChecks` now also returns `incompleteCells` (every empty day+meal cell, individually — not just "which meal types are empty all week" as before), and `canPublish()` reads it to disable the Publish button until the week is fully filled; the button's Ready-to-Publish panel itself stays a short summary line ("0 of 28 meals filled"), not a 28-line enumeration. `variety`/`runs` remain informational-only, unchanged.
  - Duplicate prevention (`timetableDnd.addItem`), no cap on items per cell, and rename-propagation via a live-name lookup (`resolveDisplayName`, preferring the Food Library's current name over a cell's possibly-stale stored snapshot) are new, small, pure-function-level rules layered onto the same write primitive — none of them needed a schema change. Library-item soft-delete (`is_active=false`, never a hard delete) already guaranteed a deleted item can't break a historical/published schedule; that was pre-existing behavior, confirmed unchanged, not newly added here.
- **Consequences:**
  - `food_schedules.source` will only ever be written as `MANUAL` going forward — `GENERATED`/`CARRIED_FORWARD` remain valid enum values (existing historical rows still use them) but nothing writes them any more.
  - Editing a `PUBLISHED` schedule is unchanged: the same row updates live, the owner's `stayoToast.undo` affordance behaves exactly as before — this refactor touched *how* an edit is composed (drag/tap vs. tap-checklist), never *what happens* once it's sent.
  - The dormant monthly voting system (`food_voting_periods`/`food_votes`, hidden since [[Decisions#ADR-056|ADR-056]]) and Food Polls (a fully separate, ad-hoc system) were untouched — the generate route's vote-tally branch is gone along with the route, but the voting tables/routes themselves are out of scope for this change, same as before.
  - No realtime/multi-tab collaboration layer was introduced — `expectedUpdatedAt` rejects a stale write rather than merging it; the owner just retries against fresh data.
- **See:** [[Food]], [[APIs]], [[Business-Rules]], [[Changelog]], [[Decisions#ADR-113|ADR-113]], [[Decisions#ADR-056|ADR-056]]

### ADR-115 — Reviews get hostel-specific categories, a standalone Overall Experience rating, and a Request Changes step; automatic categorization/sentiment stay insight-only (2026-08-25)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The resident review system shipped under [[Decisions#ADR-086|ADR-086]] used six Airbnb-shaped categories (cleanliness, food, safety, staff, **value**, **location**) with `rating` **derived** as the mean of whichever categories a resident answered — a deliberate choice at the time, reasoned as "asking a person to rate the same stay twice invites two different answers." A fuller product spec for the same feature asked for something different on both points: eight hostel-specific categories (Cleanliness, Maintenance, Food, Room Comfort, Amenities, Staff & Management, Safety, Wi-Fi — explicitly *not* Value or Location, which fit a two-night holiday-flat booking rather than a year-long hostel stay) and an Overall Experience rating the resident gives **directly**, independent of the categories. It also asked for automatic topic + sentiment detection on the free-text comment, and a four-state moderation workflow (adding `CHANGES_REQUESTED` alongside `PENDING`/`PUBLISHED`/`REJECTED`). This was scoped as an **extension** of the already-shipped system, not a rebuild — CLAUDE.md's standing instruction against duplicate parallel systems applied directly. [[Decisions#ADR-101|ADR-101]] and [[Decisions#ADR-102|ADR-102]] had, independently and slightly earlier, already fixed a signed-in resident's eligibility always reading as signed-out and made the section render nothing when there is nothing to show — this decision does not revisit either; it only reworks the review's own content (categories, overall) and adds categorization/insights on top.
- **Decision:**
  - **Category set (migration 076):** `rating_value`/`rating_location` dropped; `rating_maintenance`, `rating_room_comfort`, `rating_amenities`, `rating_wifi` added. `rating_cleanliness`/`rating_food`/`rating_safety`/`rating_staff` (now labelled "Staff & Management") carry over as columns unchanged. `food` remains the one category not always asked — unscored at a hostel with `food_included = false`.
  - **Overall Experience reversal:** `rating` is no longer derived. The resident answers it as its own 1–5 question (`ReviewsSection.tsx`'s write box asks it before the category rows), validated independently in `reviewsService.submit()` (`isValidOverall`), and Discover's headline average is the mean of these standalone answers. Each category keeps its own independent mean — never folded into the headline number.
  - **Automatic categorization + sentiment, deliberately not a model call:** `review-categorization.ts` is a pure, deterministic keyword/phrase-lexicon classifier (per-category regex lexicon, scored against a small positive/negative word list, sentence-scoped) — same "pure module, no I/O" shape as `review-summary.ts`, chosen over an LLM call for zero latency/cost on every submit and full unit-testability. Output lands in a new `hostel_review_topics` table (`review_id`, `category`, `sentiment`, `confidence`), populated inside the same `$transaction` as the review upsert, replaced wholesale on every edit.
  - **Categorization and moderation are structurally separate, not just conventionally separate:** `reviewsService.moderate()` never queries `hostel_review_topics`, and nothing in the publish/reject/request-changes path reads sentiment. A comment classified `wifi → NEGATIVE` is exactly as publishable as one classified positive — sentiment exists only to power the new admin insights endpoint (`GET /api/platform-admin/reviews/insights`), answering "what are residents talking about" as a genuinely separate question from "should this be published."
  - **Fourth status, `CHANGES_REQUESTED`:** distinct from `REJECT` in the admin's action vocabulary and in the note shown to the author, but not a new code path — editing any review already resets its status to `PENDING` regardless of what it was, so `REQUEST_CHANGES` is a softer-worded `REJECT` at the data layer, not new state machine logic.
  - **Highlights and "Resident Favourite" are derived, not stored:** `deriveHighlights()` tallies categories rated ≥4 across a hostel's published reviews into friendly tags ("Clean Rooms", "Good Wi-Fi", …) at read time; `isResidentFavourite()` is a threshold function (average ≥4.5, ≥5 reviews) over the existing summary. Neither introduces a new table — both compose data the moderation queue already produces, the same "compose, don't reimplement" discipline CLAUDE.md calls out for `financial-read-model-service.ts`.
- **Explicitly deferred, not built:** admin-configurable review categories (add/rename/enable/disable) — the eight stay a hardcoded array, matching the spec's own "hardcode for V1" instruction; a rating shown on Discover search-result cards (`HostelCard.tsx`) — out of scope, since the spec's screenshots describe the hostel listing detail page, not the search grid; server-side cursor pagination for reviews — "Show all reviews" reveals the already-fetched (up to 50) batch client-side; resident-selectable tags — tags stay purely derived.
- **Consequences:**
  - Reviews written before migration 076 have `NULL` in the four new category columns and no `rating_value`/`rating_location` data at all (those columns are dropped, not merely deprecated) — `summariseReviews`/`categoriesFor` already treat an unanswered category as absent rather than zero, so this needed no backfill.
  - Reviews written before this change have no `hostel_review_topics` rows (topics are computed only at submit/edit time, not backfilled) — the insights view will show fewer mentions for older comments until their authors next edit.
  - `stay_months` is a new snapshot column, same reasoning as the pre-existing `stayed_here`: computed once at submit time from whichever tenancy is most relevant (the active one, or else the most recently ended one), not live-joined — a stay's badge should not silently change after the review is written.
- **See:** [[Business-Rules]], [[Database]], [[APIs]], [[Features]], [[Changelog]], [[Decisions#ADR-086|ADR-086]], [[Decisions#ADR-101|ADR-101]], [[Decisions#ADR-102|ADR-102]]

### ADR-116 — The tenant Room tab shows facts or nothing, and facilities come from the owner (2026-08-26)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The Room tab had five sections, and two of them were fiction. **Living status** (water/Wi-Fi/electricity/cleaning) read `hostel_utility_status` — a table with **0 rows product-wide** and **no owner UI that writes it** — so every screen rendered its `?? 'OK'` default: *"Available · Running normally"*, asserted about a hostel nobody had ever reported on. **Room facilities** were six hardcoded literals ("Hot water 6–10 AM · 6–10 PM", "Laundry · ground floor", "RO purifier · corridor"), lifted verbatim from the design mock and true of no hostel in particular; the config file said so out loud. Only Wi-Fi read data, and **0 of 70 production rooms** had a `wifi_name`, because no owner screen existed to set one. Every facility row also rendered the same Wi-Fi glyph.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Living status is deleted**, reads and all. The table and `/api/utility-status` are left in place but unreferenced — a wrong screen costs trust, an unused table costs nothing.
  2. **Facilities come from the APPROVED marketing revision.** `AmenitySchema` gains optional free-text **`detail`** ("Attached bathroom") and **`schedule`** ("6–10 AM · 6–10 PM"). No migration — `content` is `jsonb`.
  3. **Wi-Fi is per room, unreviewed and never public**, with a new owner editor on the room sheet.
  4. **Reporting moves into the facility being reported.** The six-tile Room services grid is deleted.
  5. **A vacant bed becomes an invitation**, sharing the hostel through the existing share sheet.
  6. **Move-out gets a tenant UI** in the Room tab and on the profile's stay card.
- **Consequences / guardrails:**
  - **One source for tenant and Discover.** Both read the same approved revision, so they cannot disagree, and ADR-040 holds — an owner still cannot publish listing text unreviewed. **The tradeoff:** a tenant sees changed timings only after approval. One slow source beat two that drift.
  - **Free text, not a picker.** Hot water is "6–10 AM and evenings" in plenty of real hostels; a time picker makes that unsayable and forces a blank or a lie.
  - **Wi-Fi is deliberately not an amenity.** A password behind a review queue is absurd; a password on a public listing is worse.
  - **Reports arrive pre-categorised** — one raised from the Hot water screen needs no triage. `tenant_service_requests` (9 real rows) keeps its endpoint; owner-side handling is out of scope.
  - **An empty facilities list renders an empty state**, not six invented rows.
  - **The build's branding check earned its keep**, rejecting a `SriAdithya_5G` placeholder before it shipped.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[Bugs]], [[APIs]], [[Changelog]], [[Decisions#ADR-040|ADR-040]], [[Decisions#ADR-084|ADR-084]]

### ADR-117 — Two inboxes: hostel complaints are the hostel's, platform bugs are ours (2026-08-26)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Two report channels already existed and were never told apart in the product.
  - `tenant_service_requests` (**9 live rows**) — a resident's problem with the *building*: repairs, cleaning, food, roommates. The hostel team resolves these. Stayo stores them so the owner has one list; we do not answer them.
  - `platform_support_tickets` (**0 rows, ever**) — a problem with *Stayo*: the app, an account, a payment at the gateway. Admin-managed, with `admin_note` designed to be shown back to the reporter.

  The second had a complete pipeline — `POST /api/profile/support-tickets`, an admin queue at `/admin/reports`, a resolve endpoint, an open-count badge in the admin shell — and had never received a single report. Two reasons, both in the frontend:
  1. **The owner could not file at all.** More → Help's "Report a Bug" row called `stayoToast.info('Coming soon')`. The endpoint had been role-agnostic since it was written ("available to any signed-in profile regardless of role or tenancy"); only the button was missing.
  2. **There was no guidance layer.** The page opened straight into a form, and the owner's FAQs were `mockFaqs` — placeholder data. Someone who merely could not find a screen had a choice between filing a ticket and giving up.
- **Decision:**
  - **Keep the two tables and the two words, and make the boundary visible.** "Complaints" go to the hostel; "reports" come to Stayo. Neither surface stays silent about the other any more: the tenant's hostel-contact page gained a row to the Stayo channel, and the Stayo channel routes hostel problems back.
  - **`classifyProblem()` enforces the wall while someone types**, not after they have filed. A geyser sent to Stayo waits on an admin who cannot fix geysers; a login bug sent to the hostel waits on an owner who cannot fix logins.
  - **The nudge never blocks.** The classifier is a keyword guess, so someone certain their problem is ours can always send it. A wrong verdict costs a redirect card; a *block* on a wrong verdict would cost someone their only way to reach us.
  - **When both kinds of word appear, Stayo wins.** "The app won't let me report my geyser" is a bug in our software; the geyser is incidental to what actually failed.
  - **Guidance before a form, and the guidance is a link.** Every catalogue entry that resolves by going somewhere carries the button. Telling someone to open the Payments tab when you could open it for them is a worse answer — the same correction already applied to move-out.
  - **One component for both audiences.** Owner and tenant have the same problem shape; building it twice is how two halves drift. Only the catalogue and the hostel-channel link differ, and both come from `helpCenter.ts`.
  - **The acknowledgement stays on the page.** A toast that vanishes before it is read is not being heard. Filing shows a persistent panel, and the report joins a list carrying its status and the admin's reply.
- **Consequences:**
  - Owners can reach Stayo support for the first time. Expect the first real rows in `platform_support_tickets`, and an admin queue that now needs watching.
  - Deep links are pinned by a test against a transcribed copy of the router's table — a renamed route breaks the suite rather than quietly shipping a 404 to someone already stuck.
  - The catalogue is hardcoded, deliberately: 18 entries are cheap to edit in code and would be premature as a table with no admin editor behind it. Revisit if it outgrows browsing.
  - `mockFaqs` is now unreferenced.
- **Found while doing this:** `/tenant/complaints` had shipped a **"Report a bug" button that wrote to `tenant_service_requests`** — promising "our team will investigate" while filing a `MAINTENANCE` job with the hostel owner. **5 of the 9 live rows** came in that way. Deleted; the page now has one action and one vocabulary. See [[Bugs]].
- **Vocabulary:** a resident raises **complaints** with their hostel and sends **reports** to Stayo. The inherited word "ticket" meant both and so distinguished neither; it is gone from every user-facing string. Internal identifiers keep it — renaming those changes nothing anyone sees.
- **Rejected:** merging the two tables behind one form with a "type" field — it puts the routing decision on the person least able to make it, and would have let hostel complaints into an admin queue that cannot act on them.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Changelog]], [[Business-Rules]], [[Decisions#ADR-079|ADR-079]], [[Decisions#ADR-116|ADR-116]]

### ADR-118 — A profile shows what is missing, not what you already know (2026-08-26)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The tenant/seeker profile hub had grown to ~2,000px — roughly three phone screens — across **8 uppercase section headings** for 12 destinations. Auditing it against a live account (`speakcode`, Room 105) turned up six distinct problems, most of them additive rather than wrong:
  - **The same counts twice.** Enquiries and Saved appeared as header figures *and* as two large tiles immediately below — ~230px to say `1` and `0` twice.
  - **The same identity three times.** Name and email in the header, name again under *Personal information*, email again under *Contact details*.
  - **Four detail cards printing back what the reader already knows.** Each card carried a title, an icon, a divider and two key-value rows — ~470px to state someone's own name and birthday, and, for *Academic details*, two em-dashes.
  - **Three rows all called "Help"**, going to the Help Centre, the *public marketing* `/contact` page, and the hostel chat respectively.
  - **Three of four settings rows were `stayoToast.info('coming soon')`.** No notification-preferences screen and no password-change screen exists anywhere in the app.
  - **A heading per row.** Documents, Stayo support, Where you live and Your record were four headings over four single rows. A heading is a grouping device; over one item it is only height.
- **Decision:**
  - **Detail rows carry no values — only gaps.** The right-hand side shows `Add college & course`, or nothing. **The silence is load-bearing:** "nothing on the right" can only mean "nothing to do here" because a filled field is never mentioned. Two gaps are named rather than counted, since "2 missing" forces the tap the row exists to avoid; three or more do count.
  - **One hero, chosen by `heroMode`.** A resident with a live tenancy sees where they live; a seeker sees the portable-profile pitch; a seeker who has finished it sees neither. Never both — someone in Room 105 does not need 200px arguing for an offer they already took.
  - **8 sections → 3 plus the hero**, each with 2–4 rows. One rounded container per group with hairline dividers, not a card per row.
  - **A working professional is never told to add a college.** `detailFields` branches on `profile_type`.
  - **Dead rows are deleted, not kept as furniture.** Notifications, Language and Privacy & security come back when they exist.
  - **One Help.** The hostel channel belongs on Room, where the complaint is; a signed-in resident is not sent to the marketing site for help ([[Decisions#ADR-117|ADR-117]]).
- **Consequences:**
  - ~2,000px → ~700px; roughly one screen instead of three.
  - Settings genuinely has nowhere to live now. When notification preferences or password change ship, they need a section rebuilt — deliberately, rather than inheriting three placeholder rows.
  - The gap logic is pure and tested (`profileHub.ts`, 16 tests) against the real `speakcode` record, so "All filled" is a claim the suite checks rather than a hope.
- **Rejected:** collapsing the four detail groups behind a single "Your details ›" row. It would have been shorter still, but it puts every field two taps away and hides the gaps that are the section's whole purpose.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[Frontend]], [[Bugs]], [[Changelog]], [[Decisions#ADR-117|ADR-117]]

### ADR-119 — People own their own details, and can leave (2026-08-26)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Two gaps, opposite in kind.
  - **Changing your own phone or email needed your hostel owner's permission.** `GOVERNED_PROFILE_FIELDS = ['phone_1', 'personal_email']` routed those two through `change_requests` and an owner review queue. The table had **0 rows, ever** — the queue had never approved anything, it had only ever been a wall. A resident locked out of their own contact details is a resident who cannot receive the WhatsApp messages the product runs on.
  - **There was no way to delete an account.** No endpoint, no UI, nothing named anything close, in either app.
- **Decision — editing:**
  - **Every profile field saves directly.** The approval path, the reason box, the "awaiting owner approval" banners and the padlock glyphs are gone from the tenant side.
  - **A changed phone must still be proved**, by a code sent to the *new* number over WhatsApp — reusing `send-phone-otp`/`verify-phone-otp` and the trust rule from [[Decisions#ADR-110|ADR-110]]: a number we already reached needs nothing, a number you are changing to needs a code. Verification is the person's own job and takes one code; it is not somebody else's decision.
  - **Where WhatsApp cannot deliver, the edit saves anyway** — `verification_required: false` ([[Decisions#ADR-034|ADR-034]]) means no code is coming, and waiting for one would strand the change.
  - **Email is proved the same way**, by a code sent to the new address. There was no email OTP leg — `phone_verification_otps` is phone-shaped and a new table would need a hand-applied migration — so email codes live in **Redis** with a 10-minute TTL, which is the right store for a secret meant to expire anyway. `profiles.email` (the Supabase login identity, [[Decisions#ADR-031|ADR-031]]) is untouched; this governs `personal_email`, the contact field.
  - **Three owner gates were found in `updateTenantSelfProfile`, not one.** Beyond `GOVERNED_FIELDS` — which rejected `phone_1`/`phone`/`personal_email` outright with "require owner approval" — an `allow_tenant_edits` preference let an owner **switch off a resident's ability to edit their own profile at all**. Both are gone. `assertCapability(…, "EDIT_PROFILE")` stays: it is a move-out *state* rule, not an owner permission.
  - **The proof is re-checked server-side on the PATCH**, not merely at the verify call, so skipping the verify request skips nothing.
  - **Unchanged values are never re-proved.** Saving a form without touching the phone costs nothing, and `samePhoneValue` compares on the last 10 digits — the `7013216327` vs `+917013216327` mismatch that made [[Decisions#ADR-110|ADR-110]] inert once already.
  - **The request system is deleted, not just unwired**: `POST /api/tenants/me/profile-requests`, the owner's `/api/owner/profile-requests` approve/reject routes, the `owner-profile-requests` feature, its route and the owner Alerts card. The shared `change_requests` table and the owner-proposes-tenant-approves facade are **untouched** — that direction is live.
- **Decision — leaving:**
  - **Friction made of information, not obstruction.** The brief was to make someone think twice. The dishonest version hides the button, adds contentless steps, or refuses; it does not change minds, it manufactures resentment, and under the DPDP Act erasure is not ours to withhold. So every step *tells* them something: what specifically they lose in their own numbers, what we cannot delete and why, and the smaller fix where one honestly exists. Read it all and still mean it, and you are out in three taps. The entry sits under Log out — quiet, never hidden.
  - **`retentionOffer` returns `null` for most reasons.** Someone who has moved home will not be argued out of it. Roughly half the reasons get no offer, and that is what keeps the other half credible.
  - **A reason is required; the note is not.** Forcing prose on the way out yields "asdf" and teaches nothing.
  - **Anonymise, do not erase.** A profile is referenced by obligations, payments and agreements — a hostel's ledger is not solely the leaver's to rewrite. The row survives with nothing identifying on it; `is_active` goes false. **The flag is not the lock:** nothing in the login path reads `is_active` (verified), so the actual lock is deleting the Supabase auth user plus a Redis revocation marker.
  - **Blockers are not retention devices.** Dues, then a settlement in flight, then a live tenancy — checked server-side, because the client is where a guard would be removed. At most one is shown: three obstacles read as a wall.
- **Consequences:**
  - No migration. `system_event_logs.metadata` is jsonb, so closure feedback lands there; `profiles.is_active` already existed.
  - `/api/tenants/me/profile-requests` and the owner's profile-request queue are now unreachable from the tenant UI. Left in place rather than deleted — `change_requests` is shared with the owner-proposes-tenant-approves facade, which is live.
  - Closure is **irreversible and has never been run against a real account.** Verified by typecheck and the pure suite only.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Business-Rules]], [[Changelog]], [[Decisions#ADR-118|ADR-118]]

### ADR-120 — One list vocabulary for Discover-palette screens (2026-08-26)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The Help Centre ([[Decisions#ADR-117|ADR-117]]) and the rebuilt profile hub ([[Decisions#ADR-118|ADR-118]]) were written days apart and drifted: 36px icons in `px-4 py-3.5` rows against 30px icons in `px-3.5 py-3`, and sticky headers with different top padding. Neither was wrong — they were written twice, which is all it takes.
- **Decision:** `features/stayo-ui/ListSection.tsx` holds `SectionHead`, `ListGroup`, `ListRowItem`, `ScreenTitle` and the screen-header metrics; both screens render it. Aligning the two by hand would only postpone the next drift.
- **Why `features/` and not `shared/ui`:** these carry the Discover palette, and `check-architecture.mjs` forbids `shared/` from importing `app/`. Keeping the terracotta in one module is the same reason `discoverTheme` exists.
- **See:** [[Frontend]], [[Changelog]]

### ADR-121 — Reviews average from the first published review, not the third (2026-08-26)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** [[Decisions#ADR-086|ADR-086]] set `MIN_REVIEWS_FOR_AVERAGE = 3`, reasoned explicitly: "two reviews averaging 3.0 tells a reader nothing about a hostel and everything about two people, while carrying all the authority of a number printed next to a star." This session's Airbnb-style redesign of the Discover Reviews section ([[Features]]) made that gap far more visible — a hostel with its first (and only) review showed just a plain "Too few reviews to average yet" sentence while every design reference (and the redesign's own big-score/distribution-bars/category-grid block) implied a fuller view. Asked directly whether to (a) leave the 3-review floor and wait for more data, or (b) lower it so the very first review already renders the full view, the product owner chose (b), the tradeoff explicitly acknowledged: a single review's average is not statistically trustworthy, and the UI will now show one anyway.
- **Decision:** `MIN_REVIEWS_FOR_AVERAGE` (`review-summary.ts`) lowered from `3` to `1`. `summariseReviews()` is otherwise unchanged — the `ratings.length < MIN_REVIEWS_FOR_AVERAGE` branch (`emptyReason: 'TOO_FEW'`) is kept rather than deleted, even though it can no longer fire at the new threshold (a hostel has either 0 reviews, `NONE_YET`, or ≥1, which now averages) — deleting it would mean rebuilding the branch from scratch if this constant is ever raised again.
- **Explicitly not touched:** `isResidentFavourite()`'s own `count >= 5` floor is a separate, independent threshold (the "Resident Favourite" label needs more trust than a bare average does) and was not part of this request — a hostel can average and show its score/distribution/category grid off one review while still needing five before it can earn the favourite badge.
- **Consequences:** Every hostel's very first published review now renders the full score/distribution/category-grid block instead of the "too few to average" fallback sentence — including single-review averages that will visibly change as more reviews arrive (e.g. a lone 5★ review reads as "5.0" until a second review lands). No schema/migration change — this is a pure application-layer threshold. `tests/review-summary.test.ts` updated: the old "refuses to average one or two reviews" case is replaced with "averages even a single review," and the favourite/highlights test's `count === 2` case now asserts the review averages and highlights normally (only `isResidentFavourite` stays `false`, for the separate 5-review reason above).
- **See:** [[Business-Rules]], [[Features]], [[Changelog]], [[Decisions#ADR-086|ADR-086]], [[Decisions#ADR-115|ADR-115]]

### ADR-122 — Move-out: two lanes, a debt that survives, and a door out for the tenant (2026-08-26)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The owner-side move-out was five sequential forms — `REQUESTED → SETTLEMENT_PENDING → SETTLEMENT_APPROVED → PHYSICALLY_VACATED → COMPLETED` — walked identically whether the exit was a disputed departure with damages or the overwhelmingly common case: a course ended, the tenant already went home, the room is fine. The ceremony was priced for the 5% case and charged to everybody. Three things were worse than the slowness:
  - **Completing a move-out silently wrote off every outstanding obligation.** `confirmPaymentAndComplete` unconditionally called `obligationEngine.bulkWaiveInTx` with the reason "outstanding rent waived", behind a button the owner app labelled **"Confirm Refund & Complete"** — on a screen that had just displayed *Owed by tenant ₹25,000*. The owner tapped a button that said *refund* and forgave ₹25,000 owed to them, and was never told.
  - **The owner entered deductions blind.** The inspection form asked for damages, cleaning and missing-items amounts with no dues, deposit or balance anywhere on screen; the money first appeared one step later. `settlement_preview` was already attached to the detail response at **every** non-terminal status — the UI simply never rendered it before `SETTLEMENT_PENDING`.
  - **The tenant was deleted from the app without a word.** `hasLiveTenancy` was `INVITED|ACTIVE`, so a `FORMER_TENANT` failed `ProtectedTenantRoute` and was `Navigate`d to `/discover` — no message, dashboard tabs gone from the nav, and their own settlement record (which lives inside the dashboard tree) unreachable forever. Worse, the status flips at **`vacate`**, a whole step before the money settles at `complete`: people were evicted from the app while still owed a refund.
- **Decision — speed:** **Two lanes over one state machine.** `moveOutService.quickExit` (`POST /api/move-out/quick-exit`, owner-only) runs the whole pipeline in one action for exits with nothing left to *decide*. Every hop still goes through the same public method and therefore the same `assertTransition()`, so a request closed in one tap has exactly the audit trail of one closed in five. No new status, no weakened guard.
  - **What does *not* force the full lane: money moving.** A deposit refund or unpaid dues are normal at move-out and the one screen takes a payment mode inline. What forces the staged flow is a *disagreement* (an open dispute) or a *judgement* (damages to assess) — things needing the owner to look at the room and put numbers on it.
  - **Resumable, not atomic.** Each underlying method opens its own transaction, so a failure at step 4 leaves a consistent request at step 3 and tapping again continues from there. `planQuickExitSteps` decides what to skip; it is pure and tested, because getting it wrong fails quietly (an `INVALID_TRANSITION` from deep inside, or a step re-applied).
  - **It refuses to close an outcome the owner did not see.** The caller sends back the net and direction it displayed; the server recomputes and rejects with `409 STALE_PREVIEW`. Without this, a payment landing between the sheet rendering a figure and the owner tapping confirm would settle a different number than the one on screen. That guard is what makes one tap safe, and is the reason `expectedNet`/`expectedDirection` are required rather than optional.
- **Decision — money:** **`duesDisposition` defaults to `RECOVERABLE`, never `WAIVE`.** The tenancy closes; the debt does not. `WAIVE` still exists and still does exactly what it did — it is just no longer something that happens to an owner by accident, and when chosen the button reads *"Write off ₹25,000 & close"*.
  - **This was nearly free, because the tail risks were already handled.** Leaving obligations standing does not start a slow leak: `reminder-service` (which applies late fees) and `rent-generation-service` both filter `tenant.status = "ACTIVE"`, so a `FORMER_TENANT` accrues no late fee, gets no reminder, and has no new rent generated. `payment-service` has no tenancy-status gate, so a payment can still be recorded against them. The debt simply sits frozen and collectable.
- **Decision — the tenant's side:** **Tenancy is three states, not two** (`LIVE` / `EXITING` / `EXITED`), computed in `/api/auth/me` because only the backend knows whether an unsettled move-out exists.
  - **`EXITING` keeps the dashboard, read-only, until the settlement closes** — including the nav bar, which used to change shape the instant the bed was released. Someone owed a refund keeps the only screen that shows it.
  - **`EXITED` lands on `/tenant/farewell`**, which says what happened, shows the settlement receipt, and *then* offers Discover. That order matters: leading with "explore other hostels" reads as being swept out the door.
  - The farewell route sits **outside** `TenantProviderShell` with its own weaker guard — `ProtectedTenantRoute` redirects *to* it, so nesting it inside would redirect the screen to itself forever.
- **Consequences:**
  - No migration, no schema change. `duesDisposition` is a request field; `tenancy_state`/`exit_request_id` are computed per request.
  - **`ExitWorkflowSection.tsx` is deleted.** It was a second implementation of the same pipeline that had already drifted — it let the owner overwrite the settlement amount and direction by hand, which the sheet never did. Both surfaces are now `MoveOutSheet`.
  - **Existing callers of `POST /api/move-out/requests/[id]/complete` change behaviour**: without `duesDisposition: "WAIVE"` they no longer waive. This is the point, but it is a behaviour change to an existing endpoint, not only an addition.
  - The status vocabulary moved to `lib/services/move-out-status.ts` (no imports) because `move-out-state-machine.ts` imports `prisma`, so comparing two statuses used to open a database connection and could not be tested purely.
  - **Verified by typecheck, the pure suites (12 backend + 51 frontend new tests) and the frontend build. No part of this has been exercised against a real database or a real tenant** — `DATABASE_URL_TEST` is still unprovisioned, so `quickExit`, the `RECOVERABLE` path and the farewell screen are unrun end to end.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Business-Rules]], [[Bugs]], [[Changelog]], [[Decisions#ADR-036|ADR-036]]

### ADR-123 — The owner reads a tenant's identity; they do not edit it (2026-08-26)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The owner tenant profile's "Request Change" drawer offered four intents, the first of which was **Change Personal Information** — name, phone, email, guardian details, college. The backend's policy engine already treats these as Category B / L2 (`Rules.requiresSharedProfileApproval`), so they were never applied silently; they created a change request the tenant had to approve. But the *offer itself* is the problem: a tenant's name and phone belong to the tenant, they are shared across every hostel the tenant ever stays at via the common Stayo profile ([[Features]]), and an owner-initiated edit of them starts a negotiation nobody asked for. The drawer also computed its diffs against a four-field subset of the tenant record, so most of these fields displayed `Current: —` and the owner was proposing changes against blanks.
- **Decision:** **Personal information is removed from the owner UI entirely.** There is no owner-facing surface to edit a tenant's name, phone, email, guardian details, or college. The owner *reads* all of it — the new Tenant details card in the Stay tab renders email, phone-verified state, college/work, gender, DOB and addresses, none of which were previously shown at all despite arriving on every overview response.
- **Explicitly not changed:** the backend. `Rules.requiresSharedProfileApproval` and the Category B path stay exactly as they are. This is a decision about what the owner app *offers*, not about what the API permits — the tenant's own portal still edits these fields, and the correction window still applies to pre-activation tenants. Removing the server-side capability would be a larger change with its own migration story, and nothing currently needs it.
- **Consequences:** `ChangeRequestDrawer` is deleted (with `ChangePreview` and `PendingBanner`, dead after the swap), replaced by `AmendAgreementSheet`, which covers agreement terms only. An owner who genuinely needs a tenant's name corrected asks the tenant, or an admin does it — which is the same answer the platform already gives for a tenant's Aadhaar.
- **See:** [[Features]], [[Frontend]], [[Changelog]], [[Decisions#ADR-124|ADR-124]]

### ADR-124 — `change-rent` is the only writer of rent, and the owner UI stops offering a second one (2026-08-26)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Two live paths wrote `monthly_rent`, under opposite rules.
  - `POST /api/tenants/:id/change-rent` — identity-confirmed (password re-entry, like obligation cancel/waive), and it **reprices** every unpaid obligation at or after the effective month via `applyRentChangeInTx`. [[Business-Rules]] records this as a *deliberate, scoped exception*: "Rent changes are immediate and owner-only — no tenant approval."
  - `PUT /api/tenants/:id` with `monthly_rent` — the change-management facade classifies it Category C / L3 via `Rules.requiresContractualApproval`, **requires tenant approval**, and does **not** reprice obligations.
  The owner profile's "Request Change → Amend Agreement" used the second. So the same field had two front doors: one immediate and obligation-aware, one consent-gated and obligation-blind, with nothing in the UI indicating which an owner had walked through.
- **Decision:** **`ChangeRentModal` → `POST …/change-rent` is the single rent-writing surface in the owner app.** `monthly_rent` is not an amendable field in `AmendAgreementSheet`; the sheet instead shows the current rent and links to the rent flow, saying why it is separate ("Changing rent reprices unpaid charges — it has its own step"). `agreementAmendmentService` documents the omission at the call site so it is not re-added by someone reading only the field list.
- **Explicitly not changed:** the facade still classifies `monthly_rent` as Category C if it ever receives it. This closes the *second door* in the UI rather than removing the backend's classification, which other callers (admin tooling, the correction window) may still legitimately reach.
- **Consequences:** rent changes are consistently immediate, identity-confirmed, and reprice unpaid obligations — matching what [[Business-Rules]] already documented. The previously-possible outcome where an owner changed rent and unpaid obligations kept the old amount can no longer be produced from the owner app.
- **See:** [[Business-Rules]], [[Features]], [[Changelog]], [[Decisions#ADR-123|ADR-123]]

### ADR-125 — Rent is anchored to the tenant, not to an agreement (2026-08-26)

- **Status:** Accepted (supersedes the agreement-anchored half of [[Decisions#ADR-124|ADR-124]]; ADR-124's other half — that `change-rent` is the single rent-writing surface — stands)
- **Context:** `applyRentChangeInTx` took an `agreementId`. It read the old rent from `agreement.contract_rent`, locked the `Agreement` row, selected obligations to reprice by `agreement_id`, and the route refused the whole operation with a 404 when no suitable agreement existed. That put an **optional** record on the critical path for money:
  - `tenant_rules.agreement_required` ([[Decisions#ADR-059|ADR-059]]) lets an owner switch the signing ceremony off. Those tenants never sign, so their row stays `DRAFT` — or, where no row is generated at all, there is nothing to anchor to. Rent was simply unchangeable for them.
  - Even where an agreement existed, only `agreement-rent-schedule-service` writes `agreement_id` onto obligations. The monthly cron omits the field entirely, so cron-generated charges were never repriced and the owner was told "Rent updated" while the charges disagreed.
  - Two prior fixes (widening the accepted statuses to include `DRAFT`; widening the repricing selector with an `agreement_id IS NULL` branch) each treated a symptom. The product owner's framing settled it: *"an agreement is just a word-of-mouth between owner and tenant… we cannot depend upon that."*
- **Decision:** **`tenants.monthly_rent` is the source of truth for rent.** `applyRentChangeInTx` takes a `tenantId`; the tenant row is the anchor and the lock; the old rent is read from `monthly_rent`; obligations are selected by `tenant_id` + `hostel_id` and never by agreement. The route no longer looks up an agreement and can no longer 404 for the absence of one.
  - This matches what the codebase already believed: **every** reader of `contract_rent` falls back to the tenant (`agreement?.contract_rent ?? monthly_rent`) — `renewal-decision-service`, `renewal-offer-service`, `agreement-renewal-service`, `financial-service`, `agreement-rent-schedule-service`. The agreement was already the overlay; only the write path treated it as the foundation.
- **The agreement remains a snapshot, kept in step.** When one exists in a `RENT_CHANGEABLE_AGREEMENT_STATUSES` state, its `contract_rent` is still updated — precisely because those readers *prefer* it when present, so leaving it stale would have renewals and settlement quoting the old rent. It is best-effort and its absence is an ordinary outcome, never an error.
- **A guard had to be added, not just removed.** The agreement's `hostel_id` was what proved the caller was operating on the right property. With it gone, the service checks `tenant.hostel_id === hostelId` explicitly, and obligation selection stays hostel-scoped so a transferred tenant's charges at a previous property are never repriced.
- **Consequences:** rent changes work with zero agreements in the system, and reprice charges from both generation paths. `RENT_CHANGEABLE_AGREEMENT_STATUSES` survives but now answers a much narrower question — *which snapshot should follow?* — rather than *is this change allowed?* `CURRENT_AGREEMENT_STATUSES` is untouched and still refuses to call a draft an "Active Contract".
- **Not addressed:** rent *generation* still branches on whether a signed agreement exists (`rent-generation-service` builds `tenantsWithAgreementSchedules`), and renewals remain agreement-shaped by nature. Those are separate dependencies; this ADR covers the rent-change path only. See [[TODO]].
- **See:** [[Business-Rules]], [[Bugs]], [[Changelog]], [[Decisions#ADR-059|ADR-059]], [[Decisions#ADR-124|ADR-124]]

### ADR-126 — The tenant score follows the person, and says nothing when it doesn't know (2026-08-27)

- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** `tenantAnalyticsService.calculateTenantScore` had four faults as a credibility signal. Everyone **started at 100**, so a tenant who had never paid anything read `EXCELLENT` — the case an owner most needs warning about. It looked back **three months only**, so two years of perfect payment looked identical to three months of it. It charged a late payment **twice** — −10 for lateness and −5 per reminder, though a reminder is sent *because* the payment is late, so three reminders on one debt cost 25 of 100. And **nothing measured commitment**, so a tenant who walked out of an eleven-month agreement in month two scored the same as one who saw it through.
- **Decision — what it measures.** Two components. **Payment reliability (65)**: every resolved cycle, not a three-month window, weighted double inside the last year, with lateness banded (≤3 days, ≤10, ≤30, beyond) because those bands mean different things to an owner. **Commitment (35)**: accumulated tenure, less a penalty for stays ended before their expected length. **Reminders are not an input at all** — they remain an insight, not a second charge for the same behaviour.
- **Decision — what "early" means.** The agreement's duration when one was signed, otherwise the hostel's `preferences_config.tenant_rules.expected_tenure_months` (default 6). Agreements are optional by design ([[Decisions#ADR-059|ADR-059]]); keying commitment to them alone would leave every hostel that switched signing off with no signal, which is most of them today.
- **Decision — the penalty fades.** Proportional to the shortfall, decaying linearly to nothing over **18 months**. One departure three years ago should not outrank a year of good behaviour since, and a permanent mark would stop owners distinguishing a pattern from an incident.
- **Decision — silence over a fabricated number.** Under **3 resolved cycles** the score is `INSUFFICIENT_HISTORY`: no number, no grade, and the owner is told how many more cycles are needed. This is the direct fix for the 100-by-default problem.
- **Decision — it follows the person.** The score is computed across every tenancy the **profile** has held, not the current one. This is a **deliberate departure** from the boundary [[Decisions#ADR-110|ADR-110]]–112 drew for the document vault, where one hostel's verdict must never silently follow someone. The product owner chose it with that trade-off stated: an owner deciding whether to take a tenant on wants the person's record, and a commitment penalty that resets at each new hostel would deter nothing.
  - **The leak is narrowed to a number.** `getForOwner` returns the portable score and grade, but `insights` describe only the calling owner's own tenancy — so an owner learns how reliable someone is without reading a narrative of another hostel's tenancy. This does not undo the departure; it bounds it.
- **Decision — the tenant is told before they leave, not after.** `GET /api/tenants/me/exit-impact` returns the projected drop, produced by the same scorer the owner reads so the two cannot drift. It renders on the move-out reason step as a plain statement — not a confirmation gate, not a deterrent. Someone moving out usually has a reason that outranks a number; the point is that they were told.
- **No new table, and no cache.** Derived at read time. Caching into `tenant_behavior_scores` was the plan until it emerged that `payment-service` still writes the *old* algorithm's number into that row after every payment — a cache there would be silently overwritten by the model this replaces. Two queries on read cannot disagree with themselves.
- **Consequences:** `/api/tenants/:id/score` and `/api/tenants/me/score` both point at the new scorer, so owner and tenant see one number. `tenant-score-service.ts` is deleted. `payment-service`'s post-payment `calculateTenantScore` call now writes a column nothing reads — dead work on the payment path, left alone here because that path is not one to touch casually; tracked in [[TODO]].
- **See:** [[Business-Rules]], [[Features]], [[Changelog]], [[Decisions#ADR-059|ADR-059]], [[Decisions#ADR-110|ADR-110]]

### ADR-127 — Access is a second axis, not a redefinition of `ACTIVE`; an owner's attestation is never tenant consent (2026-08-27)

- **Status:** Accepted (Phase 1 of 2; migration not yet applied to any database)
- **Context:** `activation-workflow-service.ts` was the **only** place a tenant became `ACTIVE`, reachable only by the tenant clicking their invite link and completing registration. Every consumer that matters — rent generation, room capacity, occupancy/revenue analytics, even the owner's own manual "send a reminder" button — keyed off `ACTIVE`. A tenant who paid cash hand-to-hand and had no interest in installing anything left the owner with a permanently `INVITED` row: ₹0 owed forever, a room reading vacant with a real person sleeping in it, and a dashboard that was simply wrong for any hostel where some fraction of tenants ignored the link. The root cause: one field, `TenantStatus`, was being asked to answer two independent questions — *does this person live here and owe rent?* and *does this person have a way to log in?*
- **Decision — two axes, not one overloaded field.** `TenantStatus` keeps its existing meaning and transitions unchanged. A new, independent `TenantAccessMode` (`SELF_SERVE` default | `OWNER_MANAGED`) answers the login question. `ACTIVE` reverts to meaning only "lives here, owes rent," reachable two ways: the tenant activates (unchanged), or the owner adopts them. Every existing `ACTIVE`-keyed consumer therefore needed **zero changes** — the fix is that owner-managed tenants now actually reach that state, not that rent generation, capacity or analytics were rewritten to understand a new status.
- **Alternatives rejected:**
  - **Make every `ACTIVE` consumer also accept `INVITED`.** Rejected — it destroys a real distinction between "invited, hasn't moved in" (a prospect) and "living here without an account" (a resident), which the owner genuinely needs to tell apart.
  - **A separate offline-ledger module, parallel to the online tenancy.** Rejected — it would duplicate obligation/allocation/settlement logic that already exists and works, violating the "compose, don't reimplement" pattern this codebase otherwise follows (see [[Business-Rules#The Financial Read Model — "compose, don't reimplement"|Business-Rules]]), and it would make "invite them to the app later" a re-import instead of an attach. The chosen approach's decisive property: **the offline tenant is the same row as the online one** — same `tenant_id`, same obligations, payments, ledger and allocations, so nothing needs migrating when the tenant eventually claims their account (Phase 2).
- **Decision — the owner's assertion is recorded as an attestation, never as a `TenantPolicyAcceptance`.** Adopting a tenant, or adding one straight to the books, involves no tenant action at all — they never opened the app, never saw the rules, never clicked accept. Writing a `TenantPolicyAcceptance` row for them regardless would create a record that reads, everywhere it's surfaced, as "the tenant agreed to this" — and the one place that record is actually relied on is a deposit dispute, precisely the moment a forged one costs the most and is hardest to walk back. **A forged consent record fails exactly when it is tested.** `tenant_owner_attestations` is a structurally separate table instead: it records the owner's own assertion — who (`attested_by`), when, from what IP, an optional note — visibly a different kind of thing everywhere it's read, never mistakable for tenant consent. See [[Database]].
- **Decision — adoption is permitted where the deleted owner-side "Activate" button was not.** `InvitedTenantProfileView.tsx` documents that an earlier Activate button was removed because it let an owner flip a tenant straight to `ACTIVE` in a way that **claimed registration had happened when it had not** — silently bypassing the identity/agreement/KYC steps `ACTIVE` was otherwise a promise about. Adoption reaches the same `ACTIVE` state but makes no such claim: `access_mode = OWNER_MANAGED` is a truthful, visible statement that no registration occurred, and the attestation records that explicitly rather than papering over it. The distinction that decides this — asserting a fact about what *didn't* happen, versus fabricating that it did — is why one was safe to remove and the other is safe to ship. The invitation itself is marked `SUPERSEDED`, not cancelled, so a tenant who later clicks that same month-old link resolves to claiming the tenancy Phase 2 will build, not to a dead end.
- **Decision — reach without an account is Phase 1, not a later refinement.** An owner-managed tenant has `profile_id: null`, so every consumer that read `tenant.profiles?.phone`/`.name` directly (reminders, notifications) either skipped them entirely (`TENANT_PHONE_MISSING`) or addressed them as the literal string "Tenant." Shipping adoption without fixing this would have looked like it worked — the reminder appears to fire, logs as sent, escalates to `FINAL_NOTICE`, and then goes silent forever — while nothing ever reached the tenant. `resolveTenantName`/`resolveTenantPhone` (falling back to `tenants.display_name`/`phone_1`) and the `reminder_logs`-only-on-actual-delivery fix (see [[Bugs]]) shipped in the same phase for this reason, not as a follow-up.
- **Consequences:** `scripts/activation-invariants-check.ts` gained three `OWNER_MANAGED`-specific checks rather than having its `SELF_SERVE` checks relaxed — a genuine self-serve regression must still fail exactly as before. Four raw-SQL inner joins that silently dropped owner-managed tenants from operational queries needed separate fixes (see [[Bugs]]). Phase 2 (OTP-gated claim, the confirmation card, Revoke) is specified in the design spec but not built — see [[TODO]].
- **See:** [[Business-Rules]], [[Database]], [[APIs]], [[Features]], [[Bugs]], `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-27-owner-managed-tenants-design.md`
